<summary>This title page introduces a mystical guide by Graber and Gichtel concerning the internal "Principles" and spiritual "Worlds" present within the human soul. It promises to show the reader their own spiritual state through illustrations and descriptions of the battle between divine and worldly forces.</summary>

## Preface.

->I.<-

The beloved reader receives here, contrary to all expectation, that for which he has hoped for so many years. These are the figures of the inner man <note>original: "inwendigen Menschen" — a mystical concept referring to the spiritual or soul-centered self as opposed to the physical body</note>, which our author kept hidden for fifteen years until the end of his life; and which we have held for another ten years after his passing;

2. They would have continued to be kept secret, had not Love moved again in the spirit for the sake of its lovers <note>In this context, "lovers" refers to "Liebhaber" — spiritual seekers or those who love divine truth</note>, and finally granted permission and strengthened us to entrust them to the press. They are added to the general new printing of this blessed and expanded Third Edition of the anointed letters <note>original: "gesalbten Briefe" — "anointed" implies the letters were written under divine inspiration</note> as a lovely ornament. At the same time, they serve the reader as a key, in the spirit of understanding, to better comprehend what he reads.

3. We rejoice with you over God’s great kindness, with childlike hearts that wish each other well and praise God together for it.

4. Receive then this gift, which Love gave when the printing was already very far advanced <note>The author uses "avanciret," a Germanization of the French "avancé," meaning progressed</note>, with a modest heart as if from God; and use it, as you do all the writings of this author, for your soul’s salvation and benefit.

5. The dear reader will also, for [his] pleasant

<sig>A 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: pleasure</meta>

<summary>This preface introduces a set of mystical illustrations representing the "inner man," which had been kept secret for twenty-five years before being included in this third edition of the author's letters. The publishers present these "figures" as a spiritual key to help the reader better understand the theological depth of the text.</summary>
<keywords>inner man, spiritual figures, anointed letters, preface, mystical illustrations, soul's salvation, third edition</keywords>

# Preface.

5. take pleasure/ that we have provided a short introduction to the figures <note>The "figures" or illustrations refer to the famous mystical diagrams associated with Jacob Böhme’s followers, used to visualize complex spiritual concepts.</note> which we have incorporated into this writing at every point: We were moved to this, and disposed in spirit, when God’s Spirit opened [itself] and gave us the word to fulfill this longing.

6. This was also done in certain measure to fulfill what the Author himself was not able to set down in writing <note>original: "in die Feder fließen wollen" — literally "wanted to flow into the pen."</note>. For one cannot express the Divine riches all at once, but only as much as the Holy Spirit, who holds the key to them, grants to be spoken at any given time.

7. In that same outflow of Grace, we then also followed the process of Jesus <note>The "process of Jesus" (Proceß Jesu) is a mystical term referring to the stages of Christ's life—birth, death, resurrection—as a pattern for the believer's own spiritual transformation.</note>, what our dearest Savior has since done in our humanity, and what has been further advanced in the spirit until this time, through the power of Jesus, who is the author and finisher of our faith <note>A reference to Hebrews 12:2.</note>.

8. Praise God! that we have lived to see the day for which the Author in his time so longingly yearned. The birth-labor <note>original: "Geburts-Arbeit" — a term for the difficult spiritual struggle of bringing divine truth into the physical world.</note> with the outer spirit (which heavily affected the outer man <note>The "outer man" refers to the physical body and worldly personality, as opposed to the "inner man" or soul.</note>) surpassed the natural strength of old age; therefore the Virgin <note>In this mystical tradition, "the Virgin" often refers to Sophia, or Divine Wisdom, who guides the seeker.</note> called her Bridegroom to herself, and laid this strict and hard labor upon those of younger age.

9. In this work, we also had Jesus with us, who allowed us to enjoy the labor of the younger years of our warrior <note>A "warrior" (Streiter) refers to the spiritual seeker struggling against the darkness of the world.</note> for the common benefit of all fellow-warriors.

->To the praise of God, Amen.<-

# Preface
## To the God- and Wisdom-loving Reader:

-> I. <-

Although I have been very timid and cautious about sharing this little flower of Paradise <note>original: "Paradis-Blümlein" — in mystical literature, this refers to a spiritual insight or revelation blooming in the soul of the believer.</note> with this present coarse, swinish world—which tramples the noble pearl of the knowledge of God and of itself only with its feet, and tears those who love it to pieces—and which was opened to me in my great, earnest struggle; yet my dear fellow-combatants <note>original: "Mitstreiter" — a term for spiritual allies or fellow seekers engaged in the same "struggle" (Ringen) for divine truth.</note> have overcome me through their frequent urging to bring this to light as a guide for those who trace the Mystery of God in their innermost <gloss>innermost</gloss> ground <note>The "innermost ground" (inwendigsten Grunde) refers to the deepest part of the soul where the human meets the Divine.</note>, so that they may have a mirror before their eyes in which to behold themselves.

2. Also as a reminder and a warning, because Adam lost his opportunity <note>original: "die Schantz versehen" — an idiom meaning to miss one's chance or lose a favorable position, referring here to the Fall of Man.</note> and awakened the cold, fierce darkness within his life-forms <note>original: "Lebens-Gestalten" — the spiritual and psychological configurations that make up a person's being.</note> and made it active;

<summary>The author expresses his initial hesitation to share his spiritual revelations with a hostile world but explains he was persuaded by fellow seekers to provide this "mirror" for those looking for God within themselves.</summary>
<keywords>Spirituality, Mysticism, Inner Ground, Adam, The Fall, Paradise Flower, Spiritual Struggle</keywords>

and awakens in all its branches and children a violent struggle of evil against good, of darkness, as well as wrath and "No," against light, love, and "Yes."

3. So that everyone should therefore be on their guard, diligently and earnestly watching and praying, and also carefully maintaining a sharp watch over all the evil, poisonous influences of the hellish and earthly constellations <term>constellations: here referring to spiritual or "astral" influences that affect a person's temperament and desires</term>, and paying close attention to the pointer of the scale <note>original: "Waag-Zünglein" — literally the "tongue" of a balance scale; it represents the delicate point of decision where the soul tips toward either good or evil</note>, or the desire, pleasure, and imagination <term>imagination: in this context, the power of the mind to "form" or "image" itself into a specific spiritual state</term> of their mind, so that the good is not outweighed, and their fall and rift become even greater.

4. This is very easily and quickly overlooked if the soul becomes a little too secure and careless, imprudently turning outward toward earthly things and allowing itself to be impregnated <note>original: "schwängern" — to be made pregnant; Böhme uses this to describe the soul being filled with the spirit of the material world</note> by the spirit of this world with an earthly pleasure, or allowing a fierce thought against one's brother, or rising up in pride above the thrones <note>A reference to the sin of Lucifer, who sought to exalt himself above the divine order.</note>.

5. ...of which I have, alas! experienced sad examples in my time.

11. During this time, you are your own maker; if you remain in humility and make an angel of yourself, then you are an angel; but if you make a proud, flying devil of yourself, then you are a devil, and you cannot blame God.

12. The fire of the **eighth form** <note>original: *achte Gestalt*; in the mystical system of Jacob Böhme, this represents a pivotal stage of spiritual transformation where the soul passes through a "fire" to reach divine light</note> is the dividing-point: which figure I have indeed borrowed from Böhme’s writing *On the Threefold Life* <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a seminal German mystic; his work *De Tribus Principiis* or *The Threefold Life of Man* describes the soul's journey through different spiritual "principles"</note>, but for the sake of simplicity I have presented them in such figures [diagrams], because man has become so very earthly and external that he always speculates about what is above him, and seeks that which is very near to him inwardly in the **center of the soul** <term>center of the soul: *Centro der Seele*; the innermost point of human consciousness where the divine presence is found</term> far beyond the **starry firmament** <note>original: *gestirnten Firmament*; the physical heavens or outer space, which the author argues is the wrong place to look for God</note> in high eternity.

13. Also, in the following figures, it is painted before your eyes how the **three worlds** <note>original: *drey Welten*; likely referring to the World of Fire (divine wrath/darkness), the World of Light (heaven), and the Outer World (our physical reality)</note> are in one another, or stand under one another, just as the kind God opened them to me—yes, even led my spirit into all the **centers** <note>original: *Centra*; the foundational points of spiritual reality</note>. For I only indicate the centers: if someone wants to make large circles or **globes** <note>original: *Globos*; Latin for spheres or globes</note> and draw them into one another, he may do so; as I have seen it in the spirit, so I have also recorded it.

14. And although not everyone reaches the state of **contemplative vision** <note>original: *Schaulichkeit*; a mystical state of direct spiritual perception or beholding divine truths</note>—for it depends on Divine Grace and steadfast faithfulness, victory and overcoming—nevertheless, an earnest **warrior** <note>original: *Streiter*; a common term in 17th-century mysticism for a soul engaged in the struggle against sin and the self</note> will become well aware of these things through his own feeling and "taste" if he walks intimately with God.

15. Along with this, in the fifth chapter, I have added and explained the **battle of Michael and the Dragon** <note>referring to Revelation 12:7, interpreted here as an internal psychological and spiritual conflict</note>: what it is, and how it originates in the **creature** <term>creature: *Creatur*; the human being as a created being, often used to contrast the human with the Creator</term> through the separation and division of the **One Will** into many opposing wills; also, which temptations befall the **Virgin-son** <note>original: *Jungfrauen-Sohn*; a mystical term representing the "new man" or the Christ-like spirit being born within the believer's soul</note> before he is fully sealed in the **spiritual marriage** <note>original: *geistlichen Ehe*; the final stage of mystical union between the soul and God</note>.

16. And finally, in the sixth chapter, I have mentioned something of **prayer** and its **mystery** <note>original: *Mysterio*</note>—namely, what it is to pray rightly in spirit and truth—as I have learned it from my own experience, because it is a warrior’s indispensable sword.

17. May the dear reader receive this in love, use it for his benefit, and thank God, from whom I received it. Into whose precious love I commend the reader, and myself to your Christian prayer.

# The First Chapter.

## Preliminary Report.

->I.<-

God-seeking reader! Here, the <term>Wheel of Nature<note>original: 'Rad der Geburt'. A central concept in Boehmian mysticism describing the eternal movement and interaction of divine and natural forces that bring life into being.</note></term> is shared with you, undivided and complete according to all three principles <note>Refers to the three realms of existence in this cosmology: the dark fire-world, the light world of divine majesty, and the external world of nature.</note>, so that you may see in advance how the forms or powers of the <term>new man<note>A term for the spiritually regenerated person, often contrasted with the 'old man' of fallen human nature.</note></term> are born in their proper order within the mind.

2. Picture it vividly within your mind, and you will learn to understand our author in the following figures <note>Refers to the symbolic diagrams or copperplate illustrations that typically accompany this mystical text.</note> all the more easily, because true understanding resides within the mind.

3. Especially as he intends here, by way of introduction, to show you generally what the first-created image of God was like before the Fall—which remains hidden within the spirit, was recognized by the author through practice <note>original: 'in der Praxi'. This implies experiential spiritual knowledge rather than just book learning.</note>, and has been unfolded for you in the figures of the perfect man arising from the new birth in Christ <note>original: 'Christo'.</note>.

4. And it will gradually come into being and be recognized within you through spiritual rebirth, if you earnestly follow the writer’s path.

<summary>This page serves as an introduction to the text's mystical framework, explaining that the following diagrams and descriptions represent the "Wheel of Nature" and the process of spiritual regeneration.</summary>
<keywords>Wheel of Nature, Three Principles, spiritual rebirth, Image of God, new man, mystical practice</keywords>

--- Page 28 ---
<meta>Continuity note: This page begins the primary text of the treatise. Following the "Preliminary Report" on page 11, which introduced the Wheel of Nature and the Three Principles, the author now transitions into the "Great Mystery" of how God is revealed within the human form.</meta>

(12)

Of the Great Mystery

# Of the Great Mystery of Divine Revelation / according to Time and Eternity in the Human Image.

->I.<-

When we wish to behold and contemplate man in his inner, deep birth, we must turn with our soul out of the earthly, <term>starry and elemental life<note>Refers to the physical life governed by the celestial bodies (astrology) and the four elements of the material world, which the author considers a lower form of existence.</note></term> into the inner Divine life of Jesus Christ within us. We must call upon this <term>dear Physician<note>A common mystical metaphor for Christ, who "heals" the spiritual blindness caused by the Fall of Man.</note></term> for grace, so that He might—out of grace—re-open the eyes of those of us who are born naturally blind, which the Devil closed against us in Paradise. This is so that we may regain our lost <term>eye of light<note>original: 'Lichts-Aug'. This represents spiritual perception or the "soul's eye" that can see divine truth, contrasted here with the 'starry-eye' of human intellect.</note></term> and may recognize and behold God within us; otherwise, this will surely remain a sealed <term>mystery<note>original: 'Mysterio'.</note></term> and be incomprehensible to our rational <term>starry-eye<note>original: 'Stern-Aug'. The limited human intellect that belongs to the physical, sidereal world.</note></term>.

2. For what we see externally of man and his conduct is—aside from speech and the natural governance of sensory reason—common to all animals. And when man reaches the end of his allotted time, he goes back, just like all animals, into his mother, the Ear-

<meta>catchword: th.</meta>

3. Since it has now graciously pleased dear God to bring me, an unworthy little worm, to a <term>knowledge of myself<note>This refers to the mystical concept of 'Self-Knowledge,' where understanding one's own inner nature leads to understanding the nature of God and the universe.</note></term>, I have not buried this within my own self-interest; rather, I wish to share it for the benefit of whoever may take pleasure in it, and to present these figures within the human image. My hope is that one or another—who still thinks to behold and find God externally, above the <term>stars<note>The 'stars' or 'constellations' (Gestirne) represent the physical, astrological forces that mystics believed governed the material body and rational mind, but not the deepest spiritual soul.</note></term>—might instead catch a glimpse of himself in the spirit.

4. In my youth, I also sought this very thing with great longing, because I had read in Holy Scripture that Moses, Joshua, David, and other holy men spoke with God. I often wandered alone in the fields for half a day, looking at the heavens; yet I could not reach my goal.

5. This continued until the gracious God finally appeared to me inwardly, face to face, and opened His heaven within me. He spoke with my soul <term>essentially<note>original: 'entaliter'. From the Latin 'ens' (being); meaning a direct communication with the very essence or being of the soul.</note></term> and <term>spiritually<note>original: 'mentaliter'. From the Latin 'mens' (mind/spirit); referring to a communication with the higher intellectual or spiritual mind.</note></term>, mouth to mouth. This refreshed me in no small measure and made me very fiery with love in return for my dearest <term>Jesus<note>original: 'JEsu'.</note></term>, so that I bound myself to Him with body, soul, and spirit, never to leave Him again, nor to turn away in suffering and tribulation. I trust firmly that He will never take His Spirit from me, nor allow me to be torn away from Him.

# OF THE GREAT MYSTERY

6. Since He has also faithfully done this: To Him alone be all honor, power, strength, kingdom, and glory for all eternity, Amen!

7. Man is truly, in his external and internal birth, a **threefold constellation** <term>threefold constellation<note>original: 'dreyfaches Gestirn'. In this mystical tradition, a 'constellation' refers to a complex set of spiritual and astrological forces that shape a person’s character and physical existence.</note></term>, as can be seen from this figure; he lives in a constantly enduring drive and a hungry desire for his **center** <term>center<note>original: 'Centro'. The 'center' represents the divine spark or the original source from which the soul emerged.</note></term>, and he also anxiously rolls and turns himself around that same center to reach it and find rest within it. And even if he reaches it, he still cannot rest until he has returned once more to his first origin, from which everything was created and born.

8. The first is the **external constellation** and the ruler and leader of the natural man; it drives him to all kinds of arts, crafts, studies, and business. It makes people good and evil, rich and poor, high and low, as is plain to see. Adam fell victim to this governance in his Fall, and he brought all his children under it as well, so that in this time—without the **rebirth** and a hard struggle—we cannot entirely tear ourselves away from it.

9. This life, with its **seven forms** of external nature, winds its way into the heart, into the **sun**, which stands with its root in the fire, from which it has its brilliance and shine. Into these seven forms the devil, the old serpent, has entwined himself, and has sealed and bolted the soul, as can be read in **Revelation 5** <note>original: 'Apoc. 5'. This refers to the fifth chapter of the Book of Revelation (the Apocalypse), which describes a scroll sealed with seven seals that no one is worthy to open.</note>.

<summary>This page describes man as a "threefold constellation" driven by a restless desire to return to his divine origin. It explains how the Fall of Adam subjected humanity to the influence of external stars and how the devil has "bolted" the soul within the seven forms of nature.</summary>
<keywords>threefold constellation, the Fall of Adam, rebirth, seven forms of nature, Revelation 5, spiritual center, macrocosm and microcosm</keywords>

# Of Divine Revelation

10. The eighth form is the **Fire-World** with its fiery constellation, and it stands in the **Great World** <term>Great World<note>original: 'grossen Welt'. This refers to the macrocosm or the universe as a whole.</note></term> just as it does in the **Small World** <term>Small World<note>original: 'kleinen Welt'. This refers to the microcosm, or the individual human being.</note></term> in the center. It is the **boundary-mark** <term>boundary-mark<note>original: 'Scheide-Ziel'. A point of separation or a threshold that must be crossed to move from one state of being to another.</note></term> between the external and internal man; although in the **rebirth**, the **Light-World** is in the center, into which Jesus has placed Himself, having subjected the Fire-World within our humanity to Himself.

11. The root of this Fire-World is the **dark world** (regarding the origin of darkness, see Jacob Böhme’s *Three Principles*, chapter 21, verse 17) with its dark constellation, which holds the Fire-World captive within itself, along with all devils and damned souls. It is the boundary-mark which separates good and evil; it also **sweeps the threshing floor** <note>A biblical metaphor (from Matthew 3:12) for divine judgment, where the "wheat" of the soul is separated from the "chaff" of sin.</note> and lets nothing impure into the inner heaven to God.

12. In this Fire-World now stands the **Cherub** with his two-edged sword and guards the entrance to the inner **Tree of Life**. We must either pass through this in this lifetime, or overcome it after our time is done and experience its sharpness. For there, at the end of our life, stands **Moses** with his sharp Law, and the devil reads the **ledger of sins** <note>original: 'Sünden-Register'. A record of a person's misdeeds presented at the moment of death or judgment.</note>. Those who have experienced this can testify to it and warn all people that they should not save their repentance until the very last moment.

13. For no human tongue can express its sharpness. Oh, blessed are those who let themselves be purified in this time and have **put on Jesus** <note>A reference to the "garment of Christ," meaning to be spiritually transformed by His presence.</note>; over them the **second death** <note>A biblical concept (from Revelation) referring to eternal spiritual death as opposed to physical death.</note> shall have no power.

25. # Chapter I. Of the Great Mystery
<note>original: *Cap. I. Vom grossen Mysterio*. The "Mysterium Magnum" or Great Mystery is a foundational concept in Jacob Böhme’s work, referring to the divine chaos or the "Unground" from which the Trinity and all creation emerge.</note>

41. For the wrath of **God** <note>original: *GOtt*. The author uses all-caps to emphasize the absolute power and presence of the Creator.</note> pierces your body and soul with its sharp "No" and searches to the very bottom, to see if you stand firmly rooted in **Jesus**; and where it then finds that it cannot overcome the word **Jesus** in your heart, it finally surrenders itself and lets its sharpness sink away.

42. For the dawn <term>Morgenröhte</term> <note>original: *Morgenröhte*. An alchemical term meaning to change one substance into another; here, the "lead" of God's wrath is changed into the "gold" of His mercy.</note> the wrath into great mercy. I wish for you, dear reader, that you may feel and enjoy the taste <note>original: *Schmack*. A sensory metaphor for direct spiritual experience.</note> of this in your soul, which I am unable to express with words.

43. Now, the deeper the soul digs into itself, the closer it comes to **God**, until finally it must stand still before the Holy Trinity <term>Drey-Zahl</term>; only then does it at last attain a deep knowledge.

44. In Böhme's cosmology, these are the seven qualities or "source-spirits" that constitute both divine and worldly existence.</note> of nature; then the soul has inexpressibly more joy in such knowledge than in all the treasures of the world.

45. For what can be dearer to a mind than **God**, the eternal love? Whose sweet Spirit of Love—

46. And many a soul will curse itself on the Day of Revelation that such precious grace was so near to it, and followed it even unto the end, but was never perceived by it.

47. Because in this presented figure <term>Figur</term> <note>Referring to a symbolic diagram or spiritual illustration common in the author's works to visualize the movement of the soul.</note> the fire is placed in the eighth form <term>Gestalt</term> <note>The author describes specific "forms" or qualities of nature that lead from darkness to light. 48. To which it serves as notice that in this figure, the beginning is made from the outer nature, how human life winds and works itself from the outside inward, which is threefold.

49. Namely, the outer earthly; then the astral <term>astralische</term> <note>Refers to the "sidereal" or star-driven spirit that governs the physical body and worldly mind.</note> fire-life, both of which belong together and are the origin <term>Urstand</term> <note>original: *Urstand*. A foundational state or the original "ground" from which something emerges.</note> of the outer life of nature; and the inner fire-life with its root of darkness is the origin of the inner life of spirit or light.

## On the Natural Human.

->I.<-

When the wisdom-loving reader wishes to search for **GOD** in His wonders and behold His hidden Trinity <note>original: "Dreyheit," referring to the three-fold nature of God</note> within himself, he must, above all else, turn inward and learn to thoroughly recognize himself in his inherent threefold birth and life; for he is in himself **God’s** eternal image and likeness, according to the Dark-, Fire-, and Light-World <note>These represent the three "principles" in this philosophy: the dark abyss (the Father's power), the burning fire of the soul's will, and the light of divine love (the Son).</note>.

2. And just as these three are only one world, yet are distinct in their essences and operations, so too is the threefold life in man; and one cannot grasp the other. Each stands in its own will and fire or spirit.

3. Also, the fire of each has its own cen-

Nevertheless, we take such pride in this animal body <note>original: *Thier*, referring to the "beastly" or animalistic nature of the physical human form after the Fall</note>, decorating it with animal skins and hair, adorning it with precious stones, pearls, gold, and silver; fattening it with all kinds of dainty foods, and often losing our poor soul just to obtain such things.

36. And when we have reached the height of it, and achieved such an earthly paradise, death comes and gives the body to the earth and the worms for food, and the soul to the hellish, dark fire; which for many comes unexpectedly and too early, nor does it happen without great anxieties, as I have observed among the dying.

37. The life-spirit of this earthly man is the air, along with the seven forms—both earthly and sidereal <term>sidereal: relating to the stars or celestial influences that early modern thinkers believed shaped human temperament</term>; his vision is the sunlight; his center is eternal darkness; and where he does not turn toward rebirth <note>original: *Wiedergeburt*, the spiritual regeneration of the soul</note>, the darkness seizes him.

38. Now, because the great, starry world-spirit <note>original: *Welt-Geist*, the macrocosmic spirit of the universe</note> together with the elements provides man with food and

These fruits are sown in the seed from the start, as can be seen in the first offspring, **Cain** <note>In this theology, Cain represents the "dark" or "earthly" nature of man born after the Fall, in contrast to the spiritual potential of Abel</note>.

47. **Fire** is very good and cannot be done without: For it gives warmth to man, and also light, so that he can see in the dark; it is useful and highly necessary to him for cooking and all kinds of activities.

...thout. Love burns, useful and good, and cannot be dispensed with. For it gives man light to see in the darkness; it helps bring God’s wonders to light; it gives the light power and strength, and brings the wonders out of the darkness into existence, which would be impossible for the light alone. It awakens joy, exultation, and shouting in heaven, as well as painful torment in the darkness.

49. But if the fire wants to devour everything around it, consuming and annihilating all that is combustible, then it is evil; and where it is not extinguished, it burns as long as it finds combustible material; finally it goes out and becomes a dark coal, ash, and dust. Therefore, God also created the water, which can restrain the fire.

50. So it is with God’s **wrath-fire** <term>wrath-fire: the harsh, consuming aspect of divine power when separated from His love</term>; when it steps out of its order, breaks away from love, seeks to be a fire of its own and devour all that is good, it is evil. And if it is not extinguished, it devours all the **oily moisture** <term>oily moisture: a symbolic term for the spiritual "fuel" or sap that allows the soul to shine with divine light; without it, the spiritual light dies</term>, so that the light is extinguished, and the fire becomes a dark stump, as the reader sees before their eyes in the second figure <note>The author is referring to an illustration in the printed book</note>.

51. The symbols of the planets signify the wheel of external nature, known as the **sidereal body** <term>sidereal body: the "starry" or astral part of a human being that is influenced by the heavens and connects the physical body to the natural world</term>, which winds its way into the Sun. Around the Sun is a snake; this signifies the Devil in the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"</note>, who weaves himself into our earthly life-form, reaching even into the Sun.

### Chapter II. Concerning the Natural

52. Around the sun is a circle or globe; it signifies the **Light-World** <term>Light-World: the realm of divine love and clarity, often hidden from the natural eyes of man</term>, which however remains swallowed up or hidden.

53. And the dark globe or sphere beneath it points to the **fire-soul** <term>fire-soul: the raw, energetic core of the human spirit which can either burn with the heat of anger or the light of love</term> or God’s wrath.

54. If this dark globe or fire-world were now ignited by the Divine love-fire, so that it burned brightly and birthed a clear light in the innermost part of the heart, then the old serpent, Satan, would be cast out into the darkness.

55. And you would become an **angelic** <note>original: "englischer"; in this context, it refers to "angel-like" or "of the nature of angels," not the English nationality</note>, reborn person and a child of light, who would struggle against flesh and blood, the devil, and the world—crucifying and killing your own will with its works and business, and following Christ in the **rebirth** <term>rebirth: the spiritual transformation where the soul turns from self-centeredness to God</term>.

56. But as it is, in such a state you are only an **Anti-Christ** <note>The author uses this term not just for a historical figure, but for anyone whose spirit opposes the light of Christ</note> and an enemy of the light; you hate the light because your works are evil. Your own creaturely wrath-will—not God—makes you evil and damnable. That will can no longer be one spirit with God, nor walk and live in His commandments and footsteps, nor obey the Spirit of God, nor be the bridegroom of the heavenly **Sophia** <term>Sophia: Divine Wisdom, personified as a celestial bride who seeks to unite with the human soul</term>.

57. Rather, you are a self-willed, tame **animal-man** <term>animal-man: a person living solely according to natural instincts and physical desires, lacking spiritual awareness</term>, who desires only to live gently according to his own opinion and the comfort of the external fleshly life, without the cross or adversity,

### Chapter II. Concerning the Natural Self

...turned will, which conceives a desire in its heart and earnestly wishes to go to God in humility, [God] runs to meet him even while he is yet far off, embraces him, kisses him, and clothes him. <note>This imagery is a direct reference to the Parable of the Prodigal Son in Luke 15:20.</note>

61. Your **own will** sees clearly that if it were only to conceive a desire for God, God would immediately hasten to its aid, and would kiss it and arm it with His power; but it loves itself far too much, and fears that it might cost it its quiet, gentle life in **flesh and blood** <note>Referring to the comfortable, physical, and worldly existence.</note>.

62. And if it were to desire to break out of the **Devil’s pigsty** <note>original: "Saustall"; a vivid metaphor for the base, sinful condition of the worldly life</note>, it would lose its own **natural right**, die, and find a violent opposition in the Devil, who would assault it not only from within out of the dark **fire-root** <term>fire-root: the fundamental, harsh, and driving center of life that, without God’s light, manifests as wrath and anxiety</term> with doubt, anxiety, unbelief, impatience, and wrath;

63. But also externally it would be ambushed by his **wild swine** <note>A metaphor for worldly people who mock the spiritual path</note>, whipped, scourged, slandered, blasphemed, hated, despised, and held for an insane fool; yes, he would be nailed to the **cross** as one abandoned by God and men, and be made to cry out:

## C 5

...excuses himself with an earthly wife, and the fourth first wants to gather necessities for many years, and only then intends to become pious. <note>This continues the list of worldly excuses from the previous page, echoing the parables in Luke 14:16-24.</note>

65. **GOD**, however, does not accept such **self-will**; it is merely a resisting, dark devil within the human being, who belongs in hell and not in heaven.

66. If a person, while still stuck in **flesh and blood**, does not want to press through the anguish-fire of **GOD’s** wrath and allow his self-centeredness to be burned away, then he must nonetheless do so after casting off the earthly dwelling <note>Referring to the physical body after death</note>, where things will not proceed so gently. He who has ears to hear, let him hear!

67. **GOD** has indeed breathed into man the **free will**, along with the **seven forms** <term>seven forms: Böhme’s complex system of seven "source-spirits" or qualities that constitute both divine and natural life</term>, for the bringing forth of the eternal **Trinity** and **Divine Wisdom**; and He has ordained for him **reason** or **understanding**, with the five counsels or **senses**, as an external helper, which is called the **mind** <term>mind: "Gemüht" in the original German, meaning the deep seat of the soul’s desires, emotions, and thoughts</term>, so that the free will should rule over all creatures and reveal the wonders of **GOD**.

68. But only to this end: that he should turn inward, be subject and obedient to the Maker, and stand resigned in humility within the Divine Ground of Light, allowing [God] to work and reveal what He wills with and through him. He was meant to eat in faith from the **Word of the Lord** <note>original: "Verbo Domini"</note>, as if from the flesh and blood of the inner man, and not from the earthly fruit, as if from the outer body of flesh from the **maggot-sack** <term>maggot-sack: a blunt, 17th-century term for the physical body, emphasizing its mortality and eventual decay</term>.

# Chapter II. Of the Natural

## Chapter 5 (Continued)

69. Thus this free will of **GOD** has broken itself off from the inner Ground of Light <term>Ground of Light: A central concept in Jacob Böhme’s mysticism, referring to the deepest, divine essence of reality where God’s light shines</term> within you, made itself into its own **GOD**, and now rules with its forms of outer nature over understanding and senses; it rides upon the soul, resists and contradicts **GOD** in the innermost ground, does what it wants and what is pleasing to the flesh, and is a pure devil, a fiery dragon and serpent, which **GOD** must now wage war against, tame, and bend, crush, and cast out with His strong power of wrath.

70. Even though **GOD** already desires to come to the aid of the poor imprisoned soul inwardly through His Holy Spirit, and to strike His **Divine** Love-Fire within her, so that she might come to a bright radiance and shine, whereby the self-will could behold its own hideous dragon-form;

71. Yet this self-will—through its false desire of the flesh for lust, unchastity, honor, wealth, soft living, and foolish joy—introduces so much dampness into the soul-fire <note>The author uses the metaphor of fire and moisture to describe the soul's spiritual temperature; worldly desires act as a "dampness" that prevents the soul from igniting with divine love.</note> that it is smothered, and cannot come to a burning in **GOD’S** love.

72. Though **GOD** calls him to repentance <term>repentance: The original "Busse" implies both a change of mind and a turning away from sin</term> and turning back through His reborn children and disciples, and announces to him the presence of the **LORD**, the self-will makes excuses, claiming it is not his "gift," or that he has received another gift in which he must work the wonders of **GOD**: <note>This refers to the spiritual excuse where one claims their "talents" or "vocation" prevent them from pursuing a path of deep, humble repentance.</note>

<summary>This page describes the "self-will" as a rebellious, dragon-like force that has broken away from the divine Light and dominates the soul. It explains how worldly desires extinguish the soul's spiritual fire and how the ego makes excuses to avoid true repentance.</summary>

73. Similarly, in a house there are many kinds of wood and beams <note>original: Balcken</note>, where one serves this burden and another serves a different one.

74. Thus, he hardens himself within himself and closes the door of the mind <term>mind: The original "Gemüt" refers to the seat of emotions, will, and thought; the "inner disposition"</term> to the **Divine** voice, so that He [God] is not heard amidst his fleshly rest and rule.

75. Who will now punish and convict this beautiful "self-God"? For he is hidden within a tame beast, beneath a human mask <note>original: Larve</note>; he is outwardly pious, honorable, and religious, goes to church and to the Lord's Supper <term>Lord's Supper: The German "Abendmahl" refers to the sacrament of Holy Communion</term>, contributes to the care of teachers and the poor, boasts of Christ's baptism and of being chosen for salvation <note>original: Seligkeit</note>, or hopes to be saved through grace.

76. He knows how to speak from the Scripture <note>original: Schrift</note> as well as his teachers; indeed, he may even go so far as to leave the outward church, condemning its decline, and making acquaintance with the truly earnest disciples of Christ. He searches out their hidden life and language, circumcises himself outwardly, and abstains from his wife, or does not marry; he also speaks of **Sophia's** <term>Sophia: In the tradition of Jacob Böhme, Sophia is Divine Wisdom, the celestial bride of the soul who can only be met through spiritual purity</term> chaste marriage-bed and her union with the faithful soul.

## Chapter II. On the Natural
<meta>catchword: 82. The</meta>

...speaks like the Lamb, and yet is only an ape of Divine Wisdom, who mocks God, His children, and all righteous being.

78. He takes the sign of Jesus upon his forehead, but is only a hypocrite and a speaking mouth—not a true **deed-Christian** <term>deed-Christian: A translation of "Taht-Christ," referring to someone whose Christianity is expressed through actions and inner transformation rather than just words</term>, who carries a humble, surrendered <note>original: gelaſſenen; refers to "Gelassenheit," the mystical state of yielding one's will to God</note> will in his actions, places himself essentially into Christ’s process <note>original: eſſentialiter; meaning to participate in the life, suffering, and resurrection of Christ in one's very essence</note>, and loves the poverty of Christ—which is the only mark and touchstone of true Christians—heartily, yes, more than all the treasures of Egypt <note>original: Aegypti</note>.

79. I feel indeed the devil’s opposition in my natural selfhood <term>selfhood: The original "Eigenheit" refers to the inherent, ego-driven nature of the fallen human being</term>, because he likes to remain hidden within man and not be uncovered; but I let Him rule who opens, and no one can shut <note>A reference to Revelation 3:7, describing the absolute authority of Christ</note>.

80. For the self-will loves itself so much, and makes a person so stone-blind, that he can no longer recognize God’s love nor distinguish it from self-love; and he thinks, if one only touches his self-will with a word, that one is reaching for God in the apple of his eye <note>original: Augapfel; a biblical idiom for something most precious and sensitive</note>, and would gladly, if he only could, bring down fire from heaven to devour this enemy of his.

81. But your self-will is not God’s prophet Elijah <note>original: Elias</note>, who was subject to the Spirit of God; rather it is only a self-will of the devil, which has seated itself in your light of life <term>light of life: The "Lebens-Licht" is a term from Böhmean mysticism representing the spiritual spark or divine understanding within the soul</term>, as if in God’s temple, and rules over everything that is of God, thinking that it is God.

<meta>This page continues the discourse on the "self-will" and the "natural selfhood" discussed on the previous page. While the previous page addressed how the ego mimics spiritual language to hide its true nature, this page addresses the specific excuses "Reason" makes to avoid following Christ's path of poverty and communal love.</meta>

->Mankind.<-

82. Blind Reason <term>Reason: The original "Vernunft" often refers in this mystical context to the human intellect when it is divorced from divine revelation and used to justify worldly behavior</term> wants to speak in favor of one's own self-will and says: If all people were to walk in Christ’s footsteps and follow Him in a like-minded way, the world could not persist, because all livelihood <term>livelihood: The original "Nahrung" refers not just to food, but to the entire economic system of trade, commerce, and sustenance</term> would come to a standstill.

83. But listen, dear Reason! Six hundred thousand men, not counting women and children, went out of Egypt; yet their sustenance did not come to a standstill <note>A reference to the Exodus, where God provided manna in the desert</note>. The first Christians sold their fields and held everything in common, and yet Jerusalem persisted <note>Refers to the communal living of the early church described in the Book of Acts</note>.

84. Self-will is told that it should "possess as if not possessing" <note>original: beſitzen als nicht beſitzen; a reference to 1 Corinthians 7:30, advising spiritual detachment from material goods</note>; and where God throws wealth its way, it should not lock it away in its chests and say: "This is my property and my children's; I want to live by it and do with it as I please and understand, and leave such wealth to my children."

85. Rather, it should say: "Lord, it is Your good and the good of Your poor children; I offer it back up to You"; and it should grant the crumbs from beneath its table to the poor members of Christ <note>An allusion to the "crumbs" mentioned in Matthew 15:27</note>, and let its little brooks flow out toward them. In this way, the poor could continue unhindered and earnestly in their spiritual service to God and their prayers, and bless You, their gentle giver, in return.

86. And the teaching and life of Christ and His children is in no way the cause for the rich becoming impoverished, or for the craftsmen and their livelihoods to stand still,

## Chapter II. Concerning the Natural

[Reason] is blind and does not know Christ’s poverty, nor does it know how to distinguish it from worldly poverty: For Christ’s poor are faithful Christians who are poor purely out of love for their head, Christ, and value such poverty more highly than all the riches of the world.

96. And even if you wanted to give all your Mammon <term>Mammon: A biblical term (from Aramaic) for wealth or profit, often personified as a false god that demands total devotion.</term> to a faithful Christian, he would not accept it, because he lives in a total renunciation of all earthly goods and is a truly blessed poor man, yet rich in God; something a worldly-poor person would not do.

97. A believer does not simply make himself poor; rather, the world persecutes him, takes everything from him, and leaves him with nothing at all, banishing him from the land and thus bringing him into the true, genuine poverty of Jesus, so that he can neither buy nor sell in the world. <note>This likely refers to Revelation 13:17, a common reference in 17th-century Pietist literature to describe the exclusion of "true believers" from society and commerce.</note>

98. And thus, as if by force—yet joyful and willing—he must become Christ’s disciple and seek the Kingdom of God in firm trust that God will provide for his clothing and necessities.

99. In this faith, he must also faithfully endure without looking back <note>An allusion to Luke 9:62: "No one who puts a hand to the plow and looks back is fit for service in the kingdom of God."</note> until his end, or his faith will be taken from him, and he will have to beg and be a slave to the world and his own belly.

100. Now, if God did not care for such children and members of His household, then...

# Chapter II. Concerning the Natural [Man]

117. Reason <term>Reason: "Vernunft," personified here as a worldly, rationalistic mindset that prioritizes logic and outward action over spiritual mystery</term> says: since you speak and boast of such a high Apostolic life, why do you not also go out into the world to preach the Gospel; but instead sit quietly in corners, and do not put God’s gifts to use? <note>original: "wuchert GOtt nicht," literally "do not increase for God," an allusion to the Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25, where servants are expected to trade and grow what they were given</note>

118. 119. We have no command to go *out*, but rather to enter *in*; to this we are called, and we must stand still in the will of our Maker, whatever He wishes to do or leave undone with and through our will as His instrument <note>original: "Instrument"</note>.

120. Running and racing on one’s own initiative in "good intention" does not achieve it, and only works perversely against **GOD’s** will. A still, surrendered <term>surrendered: "gelassener," a key term in German mysticism (Gelassenheit) referring to a state of total yieldedness and letting go of self-will</term> will is more useful and pleasing to **GOD** than one's own self-active will.

121. We want solely and alone what **GOD** wants, in which we remain; and we let Reason judge us as it will, for we know that it is blind in Divine things.

122. Now we know that **GOD** is good, and everything He made was good; therefore, we can in no way blame **GOD** that we have become evil, having withdrawn our God-breathed, Divine free will from Him, the Maker, and made it our own.

123. He who is now a pure devil—whom the Spirit of **GOD** in Daniel and John [calls] abominable... <note>A reference to the apocalyptic visions of the Antichrist or the "abomination of desolation" mentioned in the Books of Daniel and Revelation</note>

129. For in the prison between time and eternity there is no agonizing torture, and it is neither dark nor light; rather, it is a twilight <term>twilight: "Demmerung," often used in mystical writing to describe a liminal or intermediate state of the soul that is neither in the full light of God nor the total darkness of the abyss</term>. 130. For my stay therein lasted only six hours; and if **GOD** had not held my tongue, I would have immediately renounced Him forever.

Now, when one considers how all religions in the world have sharpened their wits to pave a smooth path to Paradise for this **animal-devil** <note>original: "Thier-Teufel"; a term used to describe the base, instinctive, and ego-driven nature of humanity that resists spiritual transformation.</note>, and to lead it into the glory of **GOD** without the death of the **selfhood** <term>Selbheit: The "ego" or "self-will" that seeks its own way rather than submitting to the divine will; in this tradition, the self must "die" or be surrendered for the soul to be saved.</term> and the following of Christ; then one sees the **conflict of the three principles** <note>A core concept in the mysticism of Jacob Böhme, referring to the constant struggle between the dark world (fire), the light world (love), and the physical world (nature) for control over the soul.</note> for the supreme rule in the soul, how they have always wrestled with one another and stood side by side.

135. From this has arisen the blindness and great self-will of the natural man; for he believes that his own doctrine and opinion must be **GOD’s** measuring line <note>original: "Richtschnur"; a plumb line or standard used by builders to ensure a wall is straight.</note> by which He should judge, and not **GOD’s** word and commandment.

136. For here, we are not thinking of heathens <note>original: "Heyden"; historically used to refer to non-Christians or those who haven't heard the Gospel.</note> who have heard nothing of Christ: whose opinion still holds its ground among Christians to this very hour; for everyone lives and dies according to their own opinion, and thinks right along with

## Chapter II. Concerning the Natural State

...[imagined he] has to sit and reign with Christ in heaven at the right hand of **GOD**, without rebirth and the death of **selfhood** <note>original: "Absterbung der Eigenheit"; the mystical process of "dying" to one's own ego and desires to allow the divine to work within.</note>, as he is led and instructed by his blind teachers.

137. Since the great **GOD** took pity on our human misery, He has, out of His grace, through the simple, unlearned layman **Jacob Böhme** <note>A German shoemaker and mystic (1575–1624) whose complex visions of God and nature deeply influenced Western esotericism.</note>, revealed the deep ground of the three **principles** <term>Principien: In Böhme's thought, these are the three realms of reality: the dark fire-world (will), the light world (love), and the outer world (nature).</term>, as the tree of knowledge of good and evil, and the resulting fall of Lucifer and Adam.

138. This knowledge has ignited many a soul to dig further for this little pearl within themselves; then one here, another there, has found a little vein that drove them toward the love of **GOD** and prayer.

139. Although his writings only came into my hands some years after my awakening, they have been of very great use in my earnest struggle.

140. And it is indeed a matter of wonder that men so quickly departed from the great zeal of the following of Christ begun by the early Church, and fell back from the renunciation of all earthly goods into a self-serving comfort.

141. And I can never thank my **GOD** enough that He led me, the most unworthy, into the poverty of Christ itself, and unlocked its hidden nature through **practice** <term>Praxi: The lived, experiential application of spiritual truths as opposed to mere theoretical study.</term>

### Chapter II. Of Natural Things

[He] also has taught us that if we wish to be His **disciples** <term>Discipel: From the Latin 'discipulus,' referring specifically to those who follow the discipline and teachings of a master.</term>, we must likewise deny all earthly will, take our **cross** <note>original: "Creutz"</note> upon us, and follow Him. 155. The **Poverty of Christ** is the hidden treasure of all riches, powers, knowledge, and mysteries of God. A fool despises it; but a wise man holds it in high esteem. All earthly **Mammon** <term>Mammon: A biblical term for wealth or material possessions, often personified as a false god or a corrupting influence.</term> is to be considered only as filth by comparison.

162. I speak without vain glory in truth, and the Lord is my witness: If someone wanted to give me the goods of the whole world in exchange for **Christ’s poverty**, I would not want to trade with him for even a single moment: It protects against **pride** <note>original: "Hofart"; an archaic term for arrogance or haughty, "courtly" behavior.</note> and flies <note>original: "Fliegen"; likely a metaphorical reference to demonic distractions or the "flies" of Beelzebub that pester the soul.</note>, and is a fire of God that continually awakens and drives one toward love and prayer.

163. 164. God always awakens charitable hearts who take care of the believers. ## Chapter II. Concerning the Natural State

166. 167. 168. And by that very act, he lost his dominion; for thereafter, by God’s most holy will, he had to serve me and provide abundantly for my necessities. And when he noticed his loss, he pursued me powerfully and whistled most sweetly <note>A metaphor for the tempting "siren song" of worldly pleasures and comforts.</note>, but through God's grace, he could not bring me into his **Venus-heaven** <note>original: "Venus-Himmel"; a reference to a state of sensual or worldly pleasure and lust.</note>.

169. Thereupon he became violently enraged and exerted all his strength to starve out the **Poverty of Christ** <note>Here referring to the author's intentional lifestyle of humble reliance on God.</note>; but he was also wisely thwarted by God: To Him alone be eternal honor, praise, glory and thanks, power, kingdom, strength, and might. Amen!

# The Third Chapter.

## Preliminary Report.

->I.<-

In the figure shown on the opposite page—which is the second illustration by our author—it appears how faith has now made its way through the forms of outer nature into the center-point of the heart. (Included in this are the three eternal, initial forms of the <term>Center of Nature<note>original: "Centri Naturæ"</note></term>, which are understood as the world of darkness).

2. Here, Jesus rises within the heart, breaking through the dark <term>womb of nature<note>original: "Gebär-Mutter," referring to the spiritual matrix or source from which a person is reborn</note></term> and is born within us. He takes up His kingdom in our heart as the <term>Serpent-crusher<note>original: "Schlangentreter," a title for the Messiah referring to the prophecy in Genesis 3:15</note></term> promised by God. His rising and future presence are now within us without end. Through this holy Birth of Light, He continuously treads upon the head of Satan, that ancient serpent, perpetually crushing the venomous beast and casting it into the abyss.

3. Immediately, a new world opens within the will, and the prodigal son—the soul—now returns to the Father. The soul is accepted into God's heart, into the inner divine <term>Tincture of Light<note>original: "Lichts-Tinctur," a term from spiritual alchemy referring to the transformative essence of God's grace</note></term>. There, the soul puts on Jesus as a garment or a body, called the "first robe of innocence," as described in Luke 15:22.

4. Satan envies the soul for this robe, just as he envied our first parents in Paradise. He constantly confronts the soul with the fire of God’s wrath within the forms

# The Third Chapter. Of the Reborn.

<margin>The right edge of the text is obscured by the binding; missing letters have been reconstructed based on context.</margin>

...[lights] of nature in the darkness, igniting them both internally and externally; he <note>Referring to Satan, mentioned on the previous page</note> also perpetually tests the soul in the form of fire; which she <note>The soul, traditionally feminine in German ("die Seele")</note> must now firmly withstand in faith.

5. This is also a long-lasting conflict, where Love and Wrath sharpen one another, in which the Wrath must reveal the Love. It proceeds through all <term>ten forms of the inner life of fire<note>original: "zehen Gestalten des innern Feuer-Lebens." In the mystical system of Jacob Böhme, these represent the stages of spiritual manifestation from the darkness of desire to the light of majesty.</note></term> until it reaches Omnipotence. There it becomes manifest that Love alone is God and almighty, while Wrath is only Nature, which must surrender its strength and power to Love, so that from both, one single essence and being is formed.

6. In this state, the entire Godhead rises in the spirit, and God the Father and the Son embrace each other in the newly reborn humanity. There, the Father hands over humanity to the Son and presses it to His heart, marrying it to the <term>Virgin of His Wisdom<note>original: "Jungfrauen seiner Weisheit." This refers to Sophia, the divine personification of Wisdom, who acts as the spiritual bride of the soul.</note></term>, Jesus. From then on, Satan always falls like lightning from heaven to the earth and is cast out, and in every conflict, we overcome more than abundantly.

### Chapter III. Of the Reborn Human ###

...light or radiance; it is also just as impossible for our external natural eyes to behold, as it is impossible for us to look into the bright-shining sun with natural eyes.

7. And although one might wish to contrast this with Christ's appearance after His resurrection, it is nevertheless important to note that He was not yet transfigured <note>original: "verkläret." In this mystical tradition, transfiguration refers to the state where the physical body is fully permeated and transformed by divine light and glory.</note> at that time.

8. For the story of Paul near Damascus clearly shows us how Paul was so blinded by the radiation of the transfigured body of Christ that he had to go without his natural sight for three days. Not to mention my own experience <note>The author frequently references their own mystical visions as a primary source of authority, suggesting they have personally seen this divine light.</note>.

10. I can compare the powers of this body to nothing other than the colors of bright-shining gemstones: **diamonds, rubies, hyacinths, jasper**, etc.

11. These colors, playing through one another, provide such a lovely and magnificent sight that even the **angels** themselves desire to look upon it, and the human tongue is unable to express it: For we have only earthly parables, which are but a shadow compared to the Heavenly essence.

-># Humans.<-

12. Oh, how foolishly people act, that for the sake of a handful of fleeting carnal pleasure they throw away such eternal glory!

13. This body eats from the Word of **God**, or the Heavenly **Wisdom** <note>original: "Sophia." In this mystical context, Sophia represents the divine feminine mirror of God and the "spiritual meat" that sustains the soul.</note>, which shines into the body from the inner, holy fire of love and light; and desire or faith makes it substantial and tangible <note>original: "wesentlich, greislich." In the author's dialect, this refers to something becoming "graspable" or having actual substance despite being spiritual.</note>. And yet it is all only spiritual, more subtle than the air, like the **sunbeams** which penetrate through all luminous **bodies** <note>original: "Corpora."</note>.

14. This heavenly essence of the new spiritual body draws the converted soul back into its fiery **essences** <note>original: "Essentien."</note> through its strong desire—as a bridegroom draws his dear bride—and brings forth from itself a highly triumphant, bright, white light in the innermost part of the mind and soul, within the **world of light**.

15. From this, the Holy Spirit goes forth into the eternal wisdom and also helps the external life to bring forth, build, form, and create its earthly bread and necessities.

16. And this is now the holy and eternal birthing of the three **principles** <note>original: "Principien." These are the three realms of existence in Böhme's cosmology: the dark fire-world, the light-world, and the external world.</note>—as Father, Son, Holy Spirit, and Wisdom—within the reborn human; and not only bread, as you, poor blind **Reason** <note>The author frequently personifies "Reason" (Vernunft) as the limited, earthly intellect that cannot grasp divine mysteries.</note>, imagine; and you rightly ought to remain blind to this deep **mystery** <note>original: "Mysterio."</note>, because you are not worthy to know and understand it.

17. For you seek only this world, so that you may fill chests and boxes, and [increase] your abundance

### Chapter III. Of the Reborn

[...which] you might leave to your children; if you were to seek for them a pious soul, it would be much better.

18. Indeed, even the pious wealthy are blind and blameworthy in this regard, even though they already possess very good insight: in that they assist their carnal, worldly friends with hundreds <note>referring to large sums of money</note>, thereby strengthening them in their wickedness and idleness, and knowingly fattening the devil of self-interest <note>original: "Eigenheit." This refers to the egoistic "self-hood" or "mine-ness" that separates a person from God.</note> within them.

19. Since they can expect neither reward nor thanks from such godless friends, nor from **GOD**; yet they allow a mere crumb from under their table to fall to such anxious combatants in their painful [spiritual] births—which will likewise be a shame for them before **GOD**.

20. For a person will be judged not according to his good intentions, but according to his <term>Science</term> <note>original: "Scientz." In this mystical context, it refers to an internal, divine knowledge or spiritual awareness that God has awakened within the soul, rather than mere book learning.</note> opened in him by God, as the Lord Himself reveals in Luke 12:47 and James 4:17.

21. And I hope that such kind-hearted, brotherly admonition will not be so poorly interpreted; for we must let our light shine, without respect of persons, and entrust the judgment to **God**.

22. Certainly, **God** dearly loves a free will that has surrendered itself to His Ground <note>original: "Ihme zu Grund gelassenen Willen." This refers to the concept of *Gelassenheit*, a central theme in German mysticism meaning a state of yieldedness or letting go of the self to find rest in the "Ground" of God's being.</note>, wherein He Himself may will, build, form, and create whatever is pleasing to Him; He reveals Himself to such a will kindly. But He hates a self-will—

30. For poverty and need teach one to call and cry out, and to ask **God** for the strengthening of faith, which the students of **Christ** understand best.

# Chapter III. Of the Reborn

[snares] / in which I have seen very many in my time stumble and fall.

§ 59. The first temptation is this: When the soul has been cast out from the Egyptian house of bondage <term>Egyptian house of bondage; a biblical metaphor for spiritual captivity, referencing the Israelites' slavery in Egypt</term> by the spirit of this world, and has been placed into the poverty of Christ, so that it should work in the vineyard of God <term>vineyard of God; a common metaphor for the field of spiritual labor and the cultivation of the soul</term>, placing its earthly hunger in God's promises; and with its faithful helper and Soul-Bridegroom <term>Soul-Bridegroom; a mystical concept where Jesus is viewed as the spiritual husband of the human soul</term> Jesus, through its desire of faith, prayer, and supplication, it should build, form, and create its necessities in the inner heaven, so that such things are essentially cast to it on earth, and sent to it by well-doing, pious hearts:

60. If the soul then turns back into earthly reason toward Egypt, hungering for earthly bread, and takes, grasps, and seizes whatever then happens to it by chance <note>original Latin: "casualiter"</note> as a Divine providence rather than a devilish temptation; and if it does not pray diligently, standing always in fear of the tempter, and keeping sharp watch on the footsteps of Christ in the mind.

61. By this, many a brave soul has been deceived, which afterwards lamented and sighed: "Ah, if only I had not trusted! Ah, if only I had kept faith!"—though afterwards it cannot be changed.

62. For then the Heavenly Virgin <term>Heavenly Virgin; also known as Sophia or Divine Wisdom, she represents the pure, spiritual element of the soul that can be lost through unfaithfulness</term> shuts herself into her center <term>center; the innermost point of the spirit where God dwells</term>, and lets the soul knock and wait outside in vain.

# Humanity.
<meta>The previous page ended with "save her dear bridegroom with any love or..." This page continues the thought: "...let him fall into folly."</meta>

...fall into **folly**; she gives him an unruly, foolish wife to deal with <note>original: "an Hals" (literally "on his neck"). This suggests that Divine Wisdom provides earthly, domestic distractions to ground a person who is becoming dangerously obsessed with their own spiritual power.</note> / so that the great conflagration might be quenched with earthly water; also that the fiery spirit might have something to play with in its imagination / and through such a bond, its flying <note>Referring to the "high-flying" spiritual pride mentioned on the previous page.</note> might be hindered.

72. I too was awakened / and ignited by such a **fire-spirit**: For I was yet untrained / and did not know the **devil**; rather, I took him for Divine / and thought / that as an inexperienced soldier I must bow under his **direction** <note>The author uses the word "Direction," a loanword implying military or formal command.</note> / and obey his word.

73. But dear **GOD** looked upon my simplicity with **mercy** / and released me in time; for which I remain eternally grateful to Him.

74. Therefore, let the kind-hearted reader be faithfully warned: For it is much better to become wise from another’s harm / than from one’s own.

75. Indeed, certainly neither great nor small things can be born without fire / whether it be in external or internal nature. It is also true / that where there is a great fire / there is likewise a great light; which is very good and useful / if it only burns in **humility** / and does not step out of its order / wanting to devour / ignite / and consume everything.

76. Had **Lucifer** in heaven / and **Adam** in **Paradise** observed this / then the former would be a

### Chapter IV. Of the Inward Man

37. What kind of sweet taste, smell, hearing, seeing, and feeling this has given to the disposition <note>original: "Gemüt"; refers to the innermost soul and the seat of emotions and thoughts.</note> and the whole body, I cannot describe.

38. The reader must only earnestly press into the rebirth <term>Wiedergeburt: the spiritual process of being "born again" into a divine state, central to Boehme’s theology</term> and come to the wedding of the Lamb; then he will experience what transpires in the heavenly marriage, and thereafter must himself stammer, being unable to express anything of it. <note>The "Wedding of the Lamb" is a biblical image from the Book of Revelation symbolizing the joyful union of the human soul with the Divine.</note>

39. The two circles turned back-to-back are the two eternal principles <note>original: "Principia"</note> or eyes of the soul: Love and Wrath, Light and Darkness; about which one may read in Boehme’s first "Soul-Question." <note>Refers to Jacob Boehme’s *Forty Questions on the Soul* (1620), where he explores the dual nature of human consciousness.</note>

40. Into whichever principle the soul now turns itself, it sees therein, and is a stranger to the other; for it stands back-to-back against the same.

41. And no man can see further than into his mother <note>In Boehme's mystical language, "mother" refers to the source, matrix, or foundational principle from which a being's consciousness originates.</note> and speak thereof; each principle carries its own understanding, as is to be seen in Acts 12:12-13. <note>The author cites the Book of Acts here to illustrate how different spiritual states lead to different perceptions of reality.</note>

42. Now, if the reader will reflect upon the figure of the first chapter, he shall easily be able to come to the understanding of how **GOD** reveals Himself in us humans according to time and eternity, and how we have been formed into His true Divine image and likeness after and out of all three worlds.

43. It also depends solely on this: that we [turn] with our soul away from the starry reason <note>original: "gestirnten Vernunft"; the natural human intellect that is influenced by the physical cosmos or "stars," which Boehme considers limited compared to divine wisdom.</note>

# Chapter IV. Of the Inner Man /

...[it is] not good that Adam should be alone; He would make him a helper. <note>Genesis 2:18. The text begins mid-sentence, continuing the scriptural justification for the creation of Eve.</note>

49. Thus the question is: How could that which God created good have become evil, seeing that the Tree of Temptation, alongside the prohibition, had not yet been revealed? <note>The author is examining the "pre-Fall" psychology of Adam, questioning how the impulse to sin arose before the external temptation was manifest.</note>

50. Thus we must say, by virtue of Christ’s revelation in Luke 15:12, **and he divided unto them his living**, <note>original Greek: "kai dieilen autois ton bion." While this verse refers to the Parable of the Prodigal Son, the author applies it mystically to Adam's desire to divide his unified spiritual nature.</note> that Adam lusted, desired to divide the life-forms <note>original: "Lebens-Gestalten"; the spiritual configurations or qualities that constitute a being's existence.</note> and to have been separated into a woman, so that he might be equal to all the animals.

51. This soon followed, but it was against God’s first order and image; thus the Fall is not first to be sought in the biting of the apple.

52. For even though Adam had not yet eaten of the forbidden fruit with his external mouth, his <term>Imagination: in this mystical tradition, "Imagination" is not mere fantasy, but a potent, creative spiritual force of the will that "imprints" the soul onto an object.</term> entered so strongly into the forbidden tree that he was thereby overcome, and he died to the inner life of power, <note>original: "Kraft-Leben"; the vital, divine energy that sustains the spiritual man.</note> or as the Scripture says, fell into sleep. <note>Genesis 2:21. The "sleep" of Adam is interpreted here as a loss of divine consciousness.</note>

53. Now we will further investigate from where the lust for the division of the life-forms arose in Adam? We must say that it grew within him out of the <term>Principles: the fundamental spiritual dimensions—the dark fire-world, the light-world, and the external world—that exist within the human soul.</term> or life-forms themselves, and by no means out of God, who is eternal love and good, and can will no evil.

54. I have already mentioned in the previous chapter that the fire is inevitable and good,

56. 57. 58. This wrath-fire, as the dark <term>Principle: translated from the Latin "principium," meaning the fundamental source or beginning of a reality.</term>, was now the first mover toward desire in Adam, for it stood hidden within him inside the light; however, it also wished to be manifest and to be active in its own power.

59. This spirit was meant to nourish and raise Adam, but it was, as it were, swallowed up by the two inner lives and was required to be subject to them.

61. The middle one, however, as the <term>Light-Principle: the second fundamental reality of existence, characterized by divine love, clarity, and the presence of God.</term> stood in the path and held dominion over both of the others.

62. This strife and hunger for the <term>Supreme Rule: the governing power or dominance of one spiritual force over the others.</term> was now in Adam’s soul, but without compulsion; Adam could have well restrained them through the power of the Light-Principle, in which **God** alone is called **God**. <note>The author suggests that only the second principle—Love/Light—is truly "God" in the benevolent sense, while the first principle is His "Wrath."</note>

63. But what did Adam do? He <term>imagined: in this mystical tradition, "imagination" is a creative act of the will that pulls spiritual energy into a specific form.</term> so long into the earthly craving and strife of the <term>Spirit of the World: <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"</note> the animating soul or natural laws of the physical universe.</term> until he was finally impregnated and overcome; he fell into a faint, as if into a sleep, and was divided.

64. Whoever rightly understands and grasps this foundation of the Principles, to them all the hidden mysteries of the Scripture are quite clearly perceptible.

65. Seeing that from this division of the Principles, three kinds of men afterwards grew; as can be seen in the three sons of **Noah**, by whom the world was populated and spread out into three kinds of life-forms. <note>This refers to the traditional but non-biblical interpretation that Noah’s sons—Shem, Ham, and Japheth—represented different spiritual temperaments or "principles" of humanity.</note>

66. And although there are many kinds of lineages and ways of life among men in the world, including a mixed life, they all nonetheless stand under these three Principles; that is, one Principle has the supreme rule in one person over the others.

68. As Adam did then, so do we all, alas, nowadays—even including some of the <term>reborn: individuals who have undergone a spiritual awakening or "new birth" in the Spirit.</term>—and we feign allegiance to all three *Principles*.

69. 70. We serve the devil under a glistering and hypocritical cloak of prudence, wisdom, and piety, so that we do not speak the truth from the heart, nor dare to call darkness "darkness," lest it go for us as the proverb says: He who plays the fiddle of truth gets the bow smashed over his head.

71. And where we are often forced, in order to save our honor and good reputation, to step into the light, we act so wisely that no one can get under our skin; or it is said, we should not judge or condemn others, so that we are not judged in return; which indeed has an appearance of piety.

<page-num>98</page-num>
### Chapter IV. Of the Inner Man

...makes a person strong, so that he disregards any toil, labor, danger, grief, or worry, only to attain temporal and unrighteous <term>Mammon: a biblical term for wealth or material profit, often personified as a false god or a corrupting influence.</term>, honor, splendor, and pleasure;

85. In the same way, the love for Jesus makes a Christian strong to take up all suffering, cross, poverty, persecution, and tribulation, and to follow willingly after him.

86. For love makes Christ's yoke and burden gentle and light: Love drives away all fear, anxiety, and trepidation, and constantly awakens a new desire for further suffering.

87. For the person always feels comfort, joy, and strength in the heart, and experiences that God never forsakes, but rather snatches and saves one from hell, death, and distress, and also grants one victory after another.

# Chapter IV. Of the Inward Man

127. 131. And if it should perhaps seem offensive to your reason, then take up prayer and ask for the opening of Divine understanding and will, so that God may lead you in His light, so that you can see in the darkness and follow the Spirit within you in your will.

### Chapter IV. On the Inward Man

127. And indeed, one must not look with the eye of reason <note>original: "Vernunft-Aug." In mystical literature, this refers to the limited, human intellect that judges by appearances rather than spiritual truth.</note> toward the common course of the world, which exists in a governance <note>original: "Regiment." Refers to the prevailing order or rule of worldly society.</note> entirely its own and foreign to the life of **JESUS**; the eye can very easily and quickly be impregnated and led astray.

128. Instead, he must only turn his inward eye toward his predecessor and guide **JESUS** in the heart, and pay close attention to how He intends to will, work, and move within the soul.

129. And he must take great care that he does not run ahead of Him in will out of a "good intention" <note>original: "guten Meinung." A warning against self-directed spiritual zeal that is not actually commanded by God.</note>, through which many a soul has been led astray by Satan; he must only follow.

130. And this remains true even if his reason thinks it understands better, or if it seems to reason that it does not agree with the letter of the Scripture; for the Spirit of **God** understands the Scripture as He wills, not as reason perceives it, which always looks only upon the flesh.

131. And if something should perhaps seem offensive to your reason, then take up prayer and ask for the opening of Divine understanding and will, that **God** may lead you in His light, so that you might see in the dark and follow the Spirit within you in your will.

132. For you must often walk in faith where there is nothing to be seen; and you can do no better than to simply surrender your will to **God** and let Him move as He wills.

### Chapter V. Of the Battle of Michael

and will were not—by its own will—led out of the eternal science <note>original: "Wissenschaft."
