### and of the Dragon.

<margin>H 2</margin>

20. For this reason, He leads Himself out of Himself into a differentiation <term>Schiedlichkeit: the capacity of the unified Divine Will to distinguish itself into various distinct parts or "centers."</term> so that an opposition <term>Widerwärtigkeit: Literally "contrariety" or "against-ness"; the friction between opposing forces that allows for sensation.</term> may arise within that which has flowed forth, and the Good may become sensitive, active, and willing within the Evil; namely, by desiring to separate itself from the Evil and to enter again into the single will of **GOD**.

21. Because the outflowing of the single, eternal will of **GOD** always goes out from itself toward its own manifestation, the Divine Power also goes and flows out from the Eternal One into this differentiation and into many centers <note>original: "Centra." Böhme uses the Latin plural to describe the multiple points of consciousness or "source-spirits" in the created world.</note>.

22. And within the Good, He causes—through His movement—a longing to return to stillness, becoming a desire to penetrate back into the Eternal. And in such an effect stands sensitivity, knowledge, and the will.

23. **GOD**, insofar as He is called **GOD**, has nothing before or after Him that He could will; but where He wills something, that same thing has flowed out from Him and is a counter-projection <term>Gegenwurf: an "object" or "opposition-throw"; like a reflection in a mirror that allows the subject to see itself.</term> of Himself, wherein the eternal will wills within its "Something."

24. Now, if this "Something" were only a single thing, the Will would have no fulfillment <note>original: "Verbringen." This refers to the accomplishment, bringing-forth, or fruition of the will's intent.</note> therein; and therefore the groundless <term>ungründliche: "Ungrund" or "bottomless"; the state of God before any nature or light exists.</term> will divided itself in the beginning and took on a substance <note>original: "Wesen." This can mean "essence," "being," or "substance."</note> so that it might be active within "Something"; just as one has a likeness of this in the human mind <term>Gemüte: the deep-seated disposition, soul, or inner mind of a person.</term>.

25. If the mind did not flow out from itself, it would have no senses; and if it had no senses, it would also have no knowled- <note>The word "Erkenntniß" (knowledge/recognition) is completed by the catchword "niß" for the following page.</note>

<summary>Böhme explains that God's "groundless" will must differentiate itself into opposing forces (Good and Evil) to create sensitivity and self-awareness. He compares this divine process to the human mind, which requires senses and "outflowing" to achieve knowledge.</summary>

<keywords>differentiation, the Groundless, manifestation, counter-projection, divine will, human mind, opposition, senses</keywords>

### Chapter IV. On the Conflict of Michael
->and of the Dragon.<-

...ledge of itself, nor of any other thing, and could have no effect or accomplishment. <note>original: *niß*, completing the word *Erkäntniß* (knowledge) from the previous page</note>

26. The sensory outflow from the mind <term>Gemüt (the inner disposition or seat of the soul’s thoughts and will)</term> makes the mind willing or desiring to lead the senses into a selfhood <term>Ichheit (the state of being an individual "I," often used by Böhme to describe the ego's separation from God)</term>; therein the mind works with the senses, and reveals and beholds itself in the working with the senses.

27. Now, if all the senses were only one sense, they would also have only one will and would always do only one thing. How then would the wonders and powers of Divine Wisdom be recognized by the mind and brought into figures <note>original: *Figuren*, referring to the forms, shapes, or mental representations of spiritual truths</note>?

28. But because an opposite <note>original Latin: *Contrarium*</note>, such as light and darkness, is within it, the opposite itself is adverse to it; and thereby one quality always causes the other to enter into desire, to struggle against the other, and to want to rule over it.

29. In which desire the senses and the mind are led into a natural and creaturely ground <note>the fundamental basis of being a created thing as opposed to the Creator</note> toward a willing, as if toward a dominion in its own "Something"—as though it wished to rule over all others with its own sense of the mind.

30. From this, conflict and anguish, as well as aversion, arise <term>urständet (takes its origin or primal standing)</term> in the mind, so that the whole mind is thereby caused to enter again into a breaking of the senses and the self-will <note>original: *Selbwollen*</note> of the senses, and to lead itself back into **GOD**, from whom it has originated.

<meta>catchword: 31.</meta>

<summary>This page describes how the mind uses its senses to create a sense of individual "selfhood," which inevitably leads to conflict between opposing qualities like light and darkness. This inner struggle and anguish eventually drive the mind to break its own self-will and return to its divine origin in God.</summary>

31. From this arises Faith and Hope / that the anxious mind <term>Gemüt (the seat of the soul’s emotions and will)</term> hopes for a redemption / and yearns again for its origin / as for God.

32. Thus also is the Divine Revelation to be understood; for all things have their first beginning from the outflow <note>original: *Ausfluß*, meaning the emanation or streaming out of the Divine essence into form</note> of the Divine Will; whether it be Evil or Good / Love or Sorrow.

33. And yet the Will of God is no thing / neither nature nor creature; within it there is no pain / sorrow, nor aversion; but from the outflow of the Word <note>original: *Wort* (referring to the Logos or the creative command of God)</note> the understanding and knowledge have flowed forth.

34. And that same outflow is a beginning of the willing / since the understanding has separated itself into forms. Thus the forms have become desiring within themselves / to have a counterpart <note>original: *Gegenwurf*, an object or mirror-image that allows for self-reflection</note> of their likeness.

35. And this same desire was a graspability <note>original: *Infaslichkeit*, the state of being able to be contained or grasped as a concept</note> toward selfhood / as toward a "Something" <note>original: *Etwas*; Böhme uses "Something" to describe the transition from the "Nothing" of the Divine Will into the tangible world</note>; which has enclosed itself into its own willing.

36. And this own-will is now the ground of its selfhood / which encloses itself as a desiring will; it is also a ground of the darkness and of the painful sensation:

37. And it is the nature-ground / from which the multiplicity of qualities comes; so that in such adversity one will has arisen from another / to separate itself from the pain.

38. In such a breathing-out of the Divine powers into nature and creature, there are for us two kinds <note>original: *zweyerley*, the sentence continues on the next page</note>

40. For the internal will desires only a counter-reflection <note>original: *Gegenwurf*, a mirror or object that reflects the will back to itself</note> of its likeness / as a good thing / in which the good, flowed-out divine will may work and reveal itself.

41. So that same born, natural self-will also desires a likeness through its own self-containment <note>original: *Insasslichkeit*, the quality of being settled within oneself</note>, whereby it makes itself material and dark.

42. 43. And these two wills or beings are understood in two principles <note>original: *Principiis*</note>, as the reader can see in the presented figures <note>Böhme's works often included complex symbolic diagrams to illustrate these spiritual "principles"</note>.

# Chapter V. Concerning the Battle of Michael

[...of] earthly power in an eternal ground / seeks rest in fragility / but finds none.

§ 1. The great love of **God** has come to the aid of this imprisoned life and, immediately after such a fall, breathed itself back into the inner *essence* <term>Ens (Latin for 'being' or 'essence'; in Böhme's work, this refers to the spiritual substance or potential within the soul)</term>, as if into the extinguished nature of the Divine property / and gave it to life as a counter-image <note>original: *Gegenwurf*; an opposing force or reflection used to redirect the soul's focus</note>, as a new fountain of Divine unity / love and rest.

§ 2. From this [fountain], life may now draw / and its torment and unrest in the *centers* of selfhood and sensuality may be extinguished.

§ 3. Now the reader has been directed in the first figure of the first chapter / how man is a true image and likeness of **God** / according to the whole nature of all three *principles* <term>Principium (a fundamental realm; Böhme describes three: the dark fire-world, the light world of love, and the outer material world)</term> / and that **God** is much closer to him in the innermost ground than he is outside of him above the stars.

§ 4. And it depends only on this / that he stands still with the prodigal son in his own willing / working / and life / humbles himself before his **God** in the innermost ground / sets his senses and *imagination* <note>In this mystical context, 'imagination' refers to the soul's creative power to focus its desire and 'form' itself into a specific spiritual state</note> into the love of **Jesus** / calls for grace and mercy / and does not turn back or cease / until he has been perceptibly heard.

§ 5. And even if it should last from evening until morning / and even if the earthly heart itself should contradict it / he will surely experience how the dear heavenly Father will hasten to his aid / refresh his senses / embrace his mind / and kiss his fire-ground <note>original: *Feuer-Grund*; the base energy or 'burning' desire of the soul which, according to Böhme, must be transformed by the 'light' of divine love</note> with love,

71. ### Chapter V. Of Michael’s Conflict

But for one who is earthly-minded, I have written nothing; he will also not grasp it: For an earthly person lives according to the lusts of the flesh and knows of no practice other than outward occurrences <term>Zufällen (external events or superficial happenings in the material world, rather than spiritual essence)</term>.

74. A reborn person <term>Wiedergeborner (one who has undergone spiritual regeneration through Christ)</term>, however, who breaks out of the false lust of the flesh—as if from the devil’s pigsty—and places his senses within the Holy of Holies where **God** dwells and is present to the soul, will be tempted by the devil through false suggestions into the mind <term>Gemüt (the inner disposition or seat of thoughts and emotions)</term>. From these suggestions, a false *imagination*, adverse false thoughts, and resistance originate.

75. For the outward mind stands half in darkness and half in light, as can be seen in the figures of the third chapter.

76. And he must suffer much evil within himself against his will; and even though the devil cannot see into the Light-part of the soul, he nevertheless lurks in the dark part for the intentions of the will, creating a mixture and confusion in the wheel of the mind <term>Rad des Gemüts (a concept from Jacob Böhme describing the restless, rotating movement of human thoughts and desires)</term> and the senses, hindering everything good wherever he can.

77. Regarding this, Paul and all the saints have lamented at all times, that they often do what they do not want to do <note>A reference to the Apostle Paul’s struggle with sin in Romans 7:15: "For I do not do what I want, but I do the very thing I hate."</note>.

78. Therefore, if we wish to rightly understand the ground of this spiritual conflict, we must search in the light of nature <term>Licht der Natur (the innate spiritual and intellectual insight that allows a person to understand the hidden workings of God and the universe)</term> for what our mind might be? From which love and wrath, light and darkness...

# On Prayer

...nature and love were precisely drawn into the battle / from which I, like Jonah, wanted to flee. <note>Refers to the biblical prophet Jonah who attempted to run away from God’s command to preach to Nineveh, only to be redirected through a great storm and a whale.</note>

8. Because I have now learned from this / that my will and **God's** will were in conflict / and He was nevertheless too strong for me / I finally had to surrender my will completely / and let **God** rule as He wished; even though my reason could not grasp nor foresee / where **God** was aiming.

9. Until I finally, through His great mercy, after having endured the time, landed in a safe harbor / where my soul finds rest / and the taskmaster <note>original: "der Treiber." This refers to a driver of livestock or a slave-driver, used here metaphorically for the inner voices of anxiety and guilt.</note> with his whip of fear and doubt cannot reach.

10. But what it cost in sweat / earnestness, and fiery prayers / is best known to the Knower of Hearts <note>A common Pietist title for God, emphasizing His intimate knowledge of an individual's internal struggles.</note>.

11. And I am greatly rejoiced / and heartily thank the Most High at all times / who has graciously preserved for me a constant / faithful fellow-brother among so many.

12. Who faithfully watched with me in prayer / risked property and life <term>Property and life: original "Gut und Blut" (goods and blood), an idiom meaning to risk everything one owns and one’s physical existence.</term> / and has received the reward and victory of faith through **Jesus** / and can bear witness to the great mercy that has happened to us.

13. Now the Holy Spirit and one's own necessity <note>original: "Noht" (Not). The author suggests that personal suffering or desperate need is a better instructor in prayer than any book.</note> are the best masters of prayer; and we would have no need for prayer books and formulas / if we were all of one mind / and had the Holy Spirit of prayer within us.

# Chapter VI.

14. Because there are as many minds as there are heads; one seeks **God** high above the stars, another in the air, and the fewest seek and find Him within themselves; thus each goes his own way and prays according to how he is "constellated." <note>original: "constelliret." In 17th-century mysticism, this refers to a person's spiritual alignment or internal temperament, often viewed as a spiritual "horoscope" of the soul.</note>

15. An **animal-man** <term>Animal-man: original "Thier-Mensch." A person living only by natural instincts and sensory desires, lacking spiritual awakening.</term> reaches no further than the airy heaven—namely, into the **Soul of the World** <note>original: "Animam mundi." A philosophical concept of an intrinsic force that animates all nature.</note> and into the elements.

16. Others, who go somewhat deeper, penetrate into the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritum Mundi." In the mystical tradition of Jacob Böhme, this is the astral spirit that governs the physical universe.</note>, or into the starry heaven as far as the sun; further penetration is granted only to those to whom the fire is available.

17. A devilish person penetrates into the dark world; for his magic seeks only to give birth to works and words of darkness, according to the lust of his flesh and his evil will.

18. The **reborn** person, however, enters into himself with his magic, into the true holy heaven of the holy **Light-Tincture** <term>Light-Tincture: original "Lichts-Tinctur." A term from spiritual alchemy describing the divine essence that transforms the soul from a state of "dark" wrath to "light" love.</term>, until he reaches before the holy **Trinity** <note>original: "Dreyzahl," literally "Three-number."</note>; he grasps the self-subsisting, speaking Word or **Wisdom** <note>original: "Sophiam." Divine Wisdom, often personified as a feminine figure (Sophia) who acts as the soul's guide.</note> in his desire.

19. And with the **Word of Creation** <note>original: "Verbo Fiat." Latin for "the word 'Let it be,'" referring to the creative power of God's speech.</note>, he brings forth the holy **Trinity** together with Heavenly Wisdom in all his prayers.

20. And he alone worships the right, true, triune **God** in spirit and truth; and his prayer is *Yes* and *Amen* in heaven and on earth.

21. The others cling to their customary words and make forms of their own **will**—

# On Prayer.

...ing, according to the desire of the outward life, in which there is no real power. <note>The text begins mid-sentence, continuing from the previous page's description of those who pray only through their external senses.</note>

22. I have indeed heard many otherwise well-meaning people speak of their conduct, who claim that they can serve **God** and worship Him in all their outward works.

23. Which I have not wished to contradict, because not everyone knows how to distinguish between **God** and Nature, and the degrees <note>original: "Gradus"</note> of the worlds or heavens, nor understands what it is to pray rightly in spirit and truth.

24. Thus, one must have compassion for the sake of the ignorance of the simple-minded and look to **Christ**, who is our advocate with **God** our heavenly Father, and who takes pity on the blind sheep who have no proper teacher or guide.

25. Among the orthodox believers <term>Orthodox believers: original "Rechtgläubigen." In this mystical context, it refers to those holding the right internal faith rather than adhering to a specific church dogma.</term> there is still a great difference, according to whether each is yet fixed or unfixed in true love, or stands in a "moist" or deep knowledge, which is unnecessary to elaborate upon; **God** is and remains a giver of Himself, if only we desire Him with earnestness.

26. Right prayer, according to my experience and practice <note>original: "Praxi"</note>, is not making many words; but rather a sinking down <term>Sinking down: original "Ersinkung." A central concept in German mysticism (Gelassenheit) referring to the soul's meditative descent or immersion into the divine depths by letting go of the self.</term> of the soul-spirit or will into **God**, and a bringing forth of the holy **Trinity** <note>original: "Dreyheit"</note> and **Wisdom** through the seven forms of nature. <note>The "seven forms" or "seven properties" of nature are a core element of Jacob Böhme’s theosophy, describing the process by which divine light manifests through spiritual "nature."</note>

27. Which happens through magic <note>original: "Magie." For this author, magic is not sorcery but the spiritual "desire-power" of the soul that can attract and be transformed by divine influence.</note> or the desire of faith, as the will [enters] into a—

# Chapter VI.

...a <term>Magic: in this mystical tradition, "Magic" refers not to sorcery, but to the soul's innate power to manifest its desires and "will" things into spiritual reality.</term> and desire that seizes what the will wants to have and ask for. <note>The text begins mid-sentence, continuing from the bottom of the previous page.</note>

28. Because the will, however, is as thin as a thought and must have a body in which it can work, it seizes Christ's promises into the *imagination* <note>In 17th-century mysticism, "imagination" is the formative power of the mind to create an internal spiritual image or "body."</note> to be its body, and presses into God with its desire.

29. But through this desire, it becomes hard, severe, dark, and anxious, and feels within itself nothing but doubt and "No," which initially—before I understood the birth of God—*disturbed* <note>original: "turbiret"</note> me not a little.

30. For I did not want "No," but "Yes," and yet I found only more anxiety the more strongly I kindled myself in the desire, so that I even had to withdraw; but after a little while I returned again, cursed the "No" and the doubt, and took hold of a desire to penetrate into God's light.

31. There, in that outward-striving desire, the Spirit of God met me and struck up the fire in the *conjunction* <note>original: "Conjunction"; here referring to the mystical union or joining of the human will with the divine Spirit.</note>, whereby my anxious will sensitively felt itself in a lovely joy within a light, and received its answer.

32. For then all was "Yes" and "Amen," and no doubt was to be felt.

33. Through this *practice* <note>original: "Praxis"</note>, into which God led me in prayer, the foundation of **Böhme's** <term>seven forms: Jacob Böhme’s system of seven qualities or "fountains" that describe how God manifests from the void into the material world.</term> and <term>three principles: Böhme’s framework of the Dark World (divine wrath/fire), the Light World (divine love), and the Outer World (our physical nature).</term>, of which he writes in all his works, later stood open to me.

<meta>catchword: 34.</meta>

34. From this I have seen so much: that his readers, unless they are personally led by God into <term>practice: original "Praxin"; the lived, experimental application of spiritual truths rather than mere theory.</term>, will find it difficult to grasp the depth.

35. Because I have now experienced that passing through the severe wrath of God—the first dark <term>principle: in the Böhmean tradition, the "First Principle" is the dark, fiery root of God's power. Without the light of the "Second Principle" (Love), it is experienced by the soul as terrifying wrath.</term>—is a difficult path that requires not only earnestness but an undaunted courage, which nonetheless does not lie within man’s own power;

36. Therefore, let the loving follower be informed by experience that he should firmly seize love within his desire and <term>imagination: the mystical faculty of the soul to "image" or form spiritual reality within itself.</term>, and by no means let it go when in anguish.

37. In this way, he will always find courage when the wrath terrifies him or introduces doubt and unbelief; let him confidently curse these things and immediately contemplate love with his imagination; then he will certainly feel how the wrath yields and falls away.

38. In the beginning, I had to endure a hard blow, as the wrath pushed a "deadly sin against the Holy Spirit" <note>The "unforgivable sin" mentioned in the Gospels, which caused many 17th-century seekers intense spiritual anxiety (scrupulosity).</note> into my <term>mind: "Gemüt," the inner seat of the soul's emotions, will, and consciousness.</term>, making it seem as if I had cursed God.

39. Because of this, I fell into a "perverse humility," for I had not yet read <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a German shoemaker and mystic whose complex "theosophy" regarding the nature of God's wrath and love deeply influenced Radical Pietism.</note> Böhme and gained from him the understanding of what cursing, love, and wrath are—and what is God and what is not-God.

40. To an unenlightened mind, our description will certainly seem strange; even to a half-enlightened one who is not yet established in such practice, it may seem somewhat doubtful.

### Chapter VI.

41. I present to you an analogy using a seed: Behold, when it is sown into its mother <note>The "mother" here refers to the earth, but also symbolizes the "maternal" or receptive ground of the soul.</note>, it must die and pass through all <term>seven forms of nature: in Böhme’s system, these are the seven foundational qualities or "fountains" (such as contraction, friction, and fire) that drive all existence from darkness into light.</term> before it brings fruit again; this no one can deny who loves the truth in any measure.

42. For the growing power in the seed drives forward in the desire to grow, through the help of rain and sunshine; for if there were no magnetic desire in the seed, the seed would be deaf or dead and could not grow at all.

43. In the same way, the magical or magnetic desire of the soul’s will or <term>mind: "Gemüt," the deep seat of the soul's emotions and will.</term> is the creator and bringer-forth of that which the will has conceived in its <term>imagination: the mystical faculty of "imaging" or shaping spiritual reality within oneself.</term>—namely, the noble, gentle light of **God**.

44. When you have thus brought forth within yourself that beautiful, gentle light, in which the Holy Trinity dwells with Eternal Wisdom, then your whole body is indeed light, in which you have great joy.

45. But it does not last long in the outer mind; for the red dragon—the "soul-worm," or the <term>dark principle: "finstere Principium"; the foundational fiery, hungry, and wrathful source of life that exists before it is transformed by divine light.</term>—hungers fiercely for it and swallows it up again into its great magnetic hunger of wrath, seeking to quench its own thirst with it.

46. If you now wish to have your noble light shining within you again, you must simply bring it forth in yourself once more; so that in your soul a

<summary>Using the analogy of a germinating seed, the author explains how the soul must actively "birth" divine light through desire and imagination, while warning that the soul's own dark, hungry nature (the "red dragon") constantly tries to consume this spiritual clarity.</summary>
<keywords>seed analogy, seven forms of nature, magnetic desire, imagination, Light of God, red dragon, dark principle, spiritual rebirth</keywords>

# Of Prayer

46. [it] is a violent struggle between love and wrath for the supreme command.

47. This struggle always drives you into prayer and allows for little idle time; for it requires a very great earnestness.

48. This conflict lasts within you until at last the wrath, being worn out, must surrender itself to love; from this, the great mercy of **God** is born in the soul.

49. I write from my own experience and leave every other person’s experience unblamed; although I had to suffer much because of it and had to lie still under the dark, black cover of the devil, because the dragon <note>A reference to the biblical dragon of Revelation, representing the "wrathful" or "dark" principle that opposes spiritual birth.</note> arose in my brothers and always wanted to drown my noble <term>little pearl of light: "Licht-Perlein," a favorite term of Jacob Böhme representing the spark of divine life or the "pearl of great price" hidden within the soul.</term> with its stream of fury.

50. But through the earnest help of my faithful <term>Virgin: "Jungfrauen," referring to Sophia or Divine Wisdom, who in this mystical tradition acts as the spiritual bride and helper of the soul.</term>, they could not break through to victory. Eternal thanks be to **God**!

51. Now, when you have been received back by Christ into the state of Christianity and have been reborn through water and Spirit, you are a chosen priest of the Most High, to whom the right is given to enter into the <term>Holy of Holies: "Allerheiligste," the innermost chamber of the ancient Temple, here used to describe the deepest, most sacred center of the human spirit.</term> within yourself.

52. For the Word is now revealed *essentially* <note>original: "essentialiter" — meaning in its actual substance and being, rather than just as a concept.</note> in your heart and mouth, and the divine censer—namely, the spirit of prayer—is entrusted to you.

53. So that you, as a true member of Christ, may pray for all people—Jews, Turks <note>In the 17th century, "Turks" was the common term used to refer to all Muslims.</note>, and heathens—

### Chapter VI.

[...and for those] who still stand outside in the courtyard of the flesh, you should offer prayers, intercessions, and sacrifices of praise, thanks, and reconciliation; and you should present your soul as an <term>anathema: original Latin/Greek "Anathema," here used in the Pauline sense (Romans 9:3) of being willing to be "devoted" or even "cut off" for the sake of the salvation of others.</term> for theirs in Christ Jesus.

54. You must constantly wrap yourself in the love of Jesus, and through prayer, introduce this love into the wrath—into its fiery, sharp hunger—to soothe and extinguish it.

55. You must not bury your <term>spiritual talent: original "geistlich Pfund," literally "spiritual pound," referring to the Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25.</term> within your own soul, nor procure heavenly provisions for yourself alone; instead, you must always let your little stream flow out in prayer into all your **fellow-branches**, and bring your fruits of love and firstfruits to **God**; then **God** can bless you and impart new powers to you continually.

56. And by such practice, you will progress further in the inner life in one year than otherwise in many years; this, all who are experienced will have to confess along with me.

57. You do not need to go out, but only to go within, and let **God’s** Spirit go out; He will rightly perform your prayers and win over dear brides <note>In this mystical tradition, "brides" refers to other souls being awakened to the divine marriage with Christ.</note> who will thank you and **God** from the heart, because through your diligent work, salvation has come to their souls.

58. And even if it should happen that their <term>self-will: original "Eigenheit," referring to the egoistic, self-centered nature that resists divine influence.</term>—acting as the **red dragon** <note>A reference to the dragon in Revelation 12, symbolizing the prideful, wrathful nature of the fallen soul.</note> within them—refuses to surrender to love and attempts to confront you with animalistic zeal and wrath;

59. You must only remain in the love of Jesus, and never [remove] them from your <term>imagination: original "Imagination," a technical term in this mysticism for the creative power of the soul's focus and will to shape spiritual reality.</term> and will—

### Chapter VI.

...[those who] wish to exalt [themselves], he must cast down / and with the **sword of the Spirit** <note>A biblical reference to the Word of God from Ephesians 6:17</note> must hew down all enemies of **Jesus** to the left and to the right.

66. And he has very little leisure and rest in this world / but must also constantly watch with fasting and prayer / so that the devil does not triumph from the dark abyss / and swallow his noble **little pearl of light** <note>original: "Licht-Perlein," a common mystical metaphor for the spark of divine grace or the soul's inner treasure</note> / which the devil hates beyond all measure.

67. And he should in no way spend his time in idleness and laziness / as the world wrongly judges him / nor should he desire to fatten himself on the abundance of the wealthy.

68. For if he indeed desired such things / then he would have to remain in the world / make the crooked straight <note>metaphor for moral compromise or dishonesty for profit</note> / be greedy / practice usury / scrape / buy / sell / run and race / and live according to the world’s mind / so that he might have rest in the world / and peace / honor / and wealth in the flesh.

69. And he would not have to leave the world / follow **Christ** in the <term>rebirth: the spiritual transformation where the soul is "born again" into a divine nature</term> / and renounce all temporal things / down to the very shirt used to cover his shame.

70. In which life he becomes entirely foreign to all people / and walks in great earthly poverty / so that he may only reach the noble pearl of the deep knowledge of **God** and of himself.

71. Also, the devil himself covers such people with his dark cloak of blasphemy / so that they may not be recognized in this world / and steal souls away from him out of his <term>swine-pen: original "Sau-Pferch," a derogatory term for the sinful world under the devil's control</term>.

# Appendix

...more mightily and extensively treated and to be found and read; as is principally [found] in the writings of that blessed man of God, Jacob Böhme, and in the theosophical epistles of the highly-enlightened, late Gichtel: <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a famous German mystic; Johann Georg Gichtel (1638–1710) was his follower who emphasized the "Angelic" life of celibacy.</note> namely, to know that the first man, Adam, was created in the image of God with all Divine powers and attributes; so that he, with the assistance of his granted Heavenly Virgin Sophia, <note>In this mystical tradition, Sophia represents Divine Wisdom and was considered Adam's internal, spiritual partner before the Fall.</note> might have brought forth humans like himself through the <term>magic of the spirit</term> <note>original: "Magiam des Geistes"; a spiritual creative power intended for procreation without physical lust.</note> and thus gradually filled this region <note>original: "Locum"</note> of the world with God-men or angel-men, in place of the fallen spirits who had occupied it before their fall and dwelt in the light, if he had remained faithful to his Heavenly Virgin. At that time it was said: "And God saw everything that He had made, and behold, it was very good," Genesis 1:31.

2. But after the created animals and birds were presented to Adam in order to give them names according to their traits and qualities, <note>original: "Qualitæten"</note> and he saw how male and female cajoled one another and played in love, Satan infected his mind, so that he also looked around for an external wife and lusted after one who might be with him, and with whom he might also take pleasure externally.

3. His Heavenly Virgin, seeing this unfaithfulness of his—that he was deserting her—withdrew from hi-

## On the State of Marriage

...mightier and he thus became weak, losing the power to bring forth procreation out of himself; Therefore God said: It is not good that man should be alone; I will make him a helper to be with him (so that God’s purpose might be fulfilled, to populate this place <note>original: "Locum"</note> in the world instead of the fallen spirits) and He took one of his ribs and built an external woman from it, and thus separates the Male and Female **Tincture** <term>Tincture: in theosophy, a vital spiritual essence or life-force that defines the quality of a being</term> from one another. Genesis 2:18.

4. In this state, they still retained the somewhat weakened Image of God and their Paradisiacal body, which was formed from the **quintessence** <term>quinta Essentia: the purest "fifth essence" or spiritual core of matter, believed to be the substance of heaven</term> of the Paradisiacal earth. In this body, they should still have been able to bring forth their likeness from within themselves in a Paradisiacal, heavenly manner, despite the separation of the **Tinctures**. They could have reunited these Tinctures through their prayers in **Christ**, the "spoken-in" Word of God and Re-generator, if they had pressed with their will out of themselves and out of nature into God’s will. This is how God tested them with the Tree of Temptation: to see if they, through their will, would rise above themselves out of the fire into the light, where the flower of the Heavenly **Tincture** resides in **Jesus**. In that state, there is no longer man or woman, but the **Virgin** <note>This refers to the "Heavenly Virgin Sophia," the state of spiritual perfection and androgyny that the author believes Adam possessed before the creation of Eve.</note>, just as Adam was before the Fall, before the woman was taken out of him. In that state, they would have become one being in Christ through God’s will...

<summary>This page describes the mystical interpretation of the creation of Eve as the separation of Adam's spiritual essences (Tinctures) and the loss of his original ability to procreate spiritually. It explains that the "State of Marriage" resulted from Adam's weakened spiritual condition, though a path to reunification remained through divine will.</summary>

### Appendix

...how we, through the new birth in **Christ**, are [born anew], as a Paul <note>Referring to the Apostle Paul's spiritual fatherhood</note> gives birth to children for **Christ** once again; not indeed into this world, but for us who are born in the world, out of nature into **God**. **God’s** will in **Christ** is the **Divine Tincture** <term>Tincture: In theosophy, the spiritual life-force or essence that gives quality to a being, here divided into Light (love) and Fire (power)</term> according to both forms—of Light and of Fire—and **Adam’s** associated **Virgin** <note>The "Heavenly Sophia" or Divine Wisdom, considered the spiritual counterpart of the unfallen Adam</note> before the creation of the earthly woman; and in this resides our true life, Psalm 30:6 (in the Hebrew text) <note>original: "Ps. 30: 6. (im Hebräi-schen Text)"; in English translations, this usually corresponds to Psalm 30:5: "In His favor is life."</note> together with the image from **God**. Our creaturely will is also a **Tincture**, like an out-born little spark of Divine power; and when we blow upon and awaken this gift of **God** within us, so that it burns in the Light, the **Virgin** is put on by our soul, in which the marriage of **Christ** with our soul consists, and the Divine birth begins in our spirit, as Paul points to in Galatians 4:19. <note>original: "ad Gal. 4: 19."</note>

5. In nature, our will is also a **Tincture**, but it has become earthly and fleshly, into which **Satan** weaves himself. John 1:13 calls it the "will of the flesh" and "of man," and Ephesians 2:3 the "desire and will of the flesh," from which we are children of nature and of wrath. (As cited in those places). And so that **Satan** might catch **Eve**, as the weaker part, in the property of the animal <note>original: "Viehischen"; implying a beast-like or cattle-like nature compared to the heavenly</note> nature, he crept into the serpent, which was the most cunning animal in the field, as the Spirit says through **Moses**, and talked her into it with much persuasion, to reach for her own will, so that she stretched out her hand,

### Of the Married State

...and plucked the fruit from the **Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil**, and ate of it; and she gave some to **Adam** also, so that he likewise ate. In an instant <note>original: "In Instanti"</note>, the **Divine Light** was extinguished in both of them, in which the image [of God] stood in true righteousness and holiness (Ephesians 4:24), and they saw that their paradisiacal, brightly-shining **body of power** <term>Kraft-Leib: In theosophical thought, the spiritual, immortal body of light Adam possessed before the Fall, which did not require earthly food or reproduction</term> was transformed into a coarse, dark **animal-body** with beastly reproductive organs. They perceived thereby that they had fallen prey to the spirit of this world, because of which they became perplexed and seized with terror; before the wrath of **God** kindled within them, they hid themselves among the trees in the garden. There they realized that they had been deceived by **Satan**, who begrudged them their blissful state in **Paradise** and had thus led them out of it with malicious cunning. They were forced to be ashamed of one another because of the reproductive organs they had acquired and to make themselves aprons; just as we still have today, to cover ourselves before the face of the holy angels. And it is because of this deformity that our animal body must die and decay in the grave.

6. Since, following this tragic Fall, the propagation of the human race could occur in no other way than in an animal manner, the dear **God** instilled the **Crusher of the Serpent** <note>original: "Schlangen-Treter"; a messianic title for Jesus based on the prophecy in Genesis 3:15 that the woman's offspring would crush the serpent's head</note> **Jesus** as a **Re-generator** into **Eve's womb** <term>Matricem: Latin for "womb" or "matrix"; used here to denote the spiritual and physical vessel of life</term> (understand this to be in the holy element, which is above nature), so that the parents...

...might also become pregnant together in chaste desire for **GOD**; which, however, they did very weakly; and instead of bearing **GOD** (namely, the **Divine Image**) out of themselves—as **Eve** also thought, that she had received the man **Jehovah**, Genesis 4:1—**Eve** gave birth to the **spirit of the world**, ignited by **Satan**, from which **Cain** was born, the child of the grasping of one's own will <note>original: "Kind der Fassung des eigenen Willens"; this suggests Cain represented the selfish, possessive nature of the fallen human will</note>, who had **Satan** within him. 

With the birth of **Abel**, they were more fortunate, although they themselves did not know what kind of spirit was in him; therefore they gave him the name of "Vanity" or "Nothingness" <note>The name Abel is derived from the Hebrew *Hevel*, meaning breath or vapor, often translated in the Bible as "vanity" to denote something fleeting or insignificant</note>, as one who is miserable; and he was indeed like a "nothing" in his nature. Such children are very rare who are baptized by the **Holy Spirit** in the mother's womb, and among thousands, one is hardly found, unless **GOD** beholds them in the goal of His **Covenant of Grace** <term>Gnaden-Bund: A theological term for the promise of salvation made by God to humanity</term>, as in **John the Baptist** in the New Testament, who was to be a forerunner of **JESUS**. 

These children are conformed to **JESUS**, whom **GOD** baptizes with heavenly fire, so that nature becomes a hell to them; therefore they do not live according to the flesh, but according to the Spirit, which is why **Abel** also took no wife, just as **John** did not. The **Patriarchs** <term>Ertz-Väter: The ancestral fathers of the faith, such as Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob</term>, from whom **JESUS** was to be born according to the flesh, were therefore required to have wives; although **GOD** certainly "salted their flesh sharply" <note>original: "GOTT das Fleisch wol scharf gesaltzen hat"; an idiomatic way of saying God disciplined their earthly desires through trials and suffering</note>. # On the State of Marriage.

...He supports us in nature as a Father, so that we may attain His holiness and not be condemned with the ungodly world. Therefore, it is well to consider here what the state of marriage <term>Ehe-Stand: The divinely ordained institution of marriage, seen here in both its natural and spiritual aspects</term> involves, which has as its foundation the procreation of the human race, Genesis 1:28 and 8:17. And to this extent, it is borne under Divine patience and recognized as His ordinance according to nature following the Fall of man (but not according to the kingdom of grace. See <note>original: "vid." for the Latin *vide*</note> Psalm 51:7, John 3:3, 6, Ephesians 2:3). 

However, the abuse of the state of marriage—where man seeks to exercise his animalistic lust and the lewdness of the sinful flesh—is of Satan, the old serpent, and is whoredom before God. In this, sinful man is worse than the dumb, irrational beasts, which, once they have conceived, immediately lose (extinguish) all heat <note>original: "Brunst"; referring to the animal mating instinct</note>, and the male no longer desires the female, nor she the male (unless it be a bull or similar very coarse cattle). Thus, man rightfully ought to be ashamed before the animals, for he has become lower than they. This consideration must drive him to moderate himself in nature and to pursue discipline, chastity, and purity with all his strength, and to apply himself to these virtues.

# Of the State of Marriage

our dear <term>Savior: "Heyland," a common Pietist term for Jesus as the one who heals and saves</term>—who in all things, except for sin, became like us—as a <term>prototype: "Fürbild," a biblical model or type</term> of holiness and purity, took no wife.

13. And because we then, according to the teaching and example of our dear Savior, should love God with all our heart, with all our soul, with all our mind, and with all our strength (*Matthew* 22:37, *Mark* 12:30), and should also be holy and perfect, just as the Father in heaven is perfect and holy (*Matthew* 5:48, *1 Peter* 1:14-16), and since without holiness no one shall see God, and we should therefore pursue the same (*Hebrews* 12:14); so indeed is such a God-loving soul in no way to be blamed, nor held in contempt, if—enkindled in the mind by the light of God—it seeks its own sanctification. Such a soul seeks to follow Jesus Christ in <term>rebirth: "Wiedergeburt," a central concept in Pietism referring to a spiritual awakening and inner transformation</term> and the renewal of the person through the drawing of the Father, and "circumcises" itself for the sake of the kingdom of heaven <note>original: "sich... beschneidet"; a reference to the "eunuchs for the sake of the kingdom" in Matthew 19:12</note>. Therefore, it may also hesitate in conscience to fulfill the otherwise permissible duty of <term>marital intercourse: "Ehestandlichen Beyschlafens," literally the duty of sleeping beside one another in the marriage state</term>; and even less so to fulfill the unchaste, impure, indecent, lustful, and animalistic nature and will of its spouse. And here, no regard for nature can take place; rather, it is said—above all that which has already been stated in <note>The author refers back to an earlier section of this treatise</note> No. 8—according to the pronouncement of the dear Savior himself: Let the dead bury their dead, but you follow me (*Matthew* 
->8:22.<-)

<summary>This page argues that since Jesus remained celibate as a model of holiness, devout Christians may feel a spiritual calling to abstain from sexual relations within marriage to pursue a higher state of sanctification.</summary>

<page-num>171</page-num>
# On the State of Marriage.

...our dear <term>Savior: "Heyland," a common Pietist term for Jesus as the one who heals and saves</term>—who in all things, except for sin, became like us—as a model of holiness and purity, took no wife.

13. And since we, according to the teaching and example of our dear Savior, are to love God with all our heart, with all our soul, with all our mind, and with all our strength (*Matthew 22:37, Mark 12:30*), and also be holy and perfect, even as the Father in heaven is perfect and holy (*Matthew 5:48, 1 Peter 1:14-16*), and since without <term>sanctification: "Heiligung," the process of becoming holy or set apart for God</term> no one shall see God, and we should therefore pursue the same (*Hebrews 12:14*); therefore, such a God-loving soul is in no way to be blamed or criticized if, kindled by the light of God in their mind, they seek their sanctification. In order to follow Jesus Christ in the <term>rebirth: "Wiedergeburt," a central Pietist concept of spiritual renewal</term> and the renewal of man through the drawing of the Father, such a soul <note>original: "sich... beschneidet," literally "circumcises itself," but here referencing Matthew 19:12 regarding those who "make themselves eunuchs" for the sake of the kingdom of heaven</note> sets itself apart for the sake of the kingdom of heaven. Therefore, it may happen that their conscience hesitates to fulfill the otherwise permissible duty of <term>marital intercourse: "Ehestandlichen Beyschlafens," literally "marital sleeping-beside"</term>; much less are they willing to satisfy the unchaste, impure, indecent, lustful, and animalistic nature and will of their spouse. And here, no "regard for nature" <note>The author argues that natural biological urges or social expectations of marriage should not outweigh spiritual convictions</note> can take place; instead, it is rather a matter of everything that has already been said above in number 8, and also according to the saying of the dear Savior himself: *Let the dead bury their dead, but you follow me,* Matthew
<meta>8:22.</meta>

<summary>This page argues that devout Christians should emulate Jesus’s celibacy to achieve true holiness, suggesting that a spiritually "reborn" person may feel a religious duty to abstain from sexual relations even within marriage.</summary>
<keywords>celibacy, sanctification, imitation of Christ, marriage duties, rebirth, Matthew 19:12, asceticism</keywords>

O shame upon shame! # On the Marital State

door is, to turn away the ungodly nature in Jacob—that is, His Christendom—as a <term>Goël: a Hebrew term for a "kinsman-redeemer," a protector who according to Mosaic law had the right to redeem a relative from debt or slavery, or to avenge them</term> and strong deliverer of His own, who turn to Him with their whole heart, *Isaiah 59:20; Romans 11:26*, and cry to Him day and night in their fears and distresses, *Luke 18:7*. He has already appeared in His saints and has washed them clean from their filth with His blood, *Revelation 1:5*, in fulfillment of the prophecy in *Isaiah 4:4*. And it is this blood that cries out to **GOD** in heaven regarding the violence and distress caused by Satan, the hellish serpent; therefore, the dear **GOD** cannot refrain from helping the <term>heavenly mother: likely a reference to the New Jerusalem or the spiritual Church in labor to bring forth the "new man"</term> out of her great labor, until she will finally give birth to her children gently and without pain, *Isaiah 66:8*. Oh come, dear **Lord JESUS**, soon: Amen, Amen!

15. But if one should ask how one is actually to behave toward those spouses, on one side or the other, who are drowned in their carnal lusts and want to know nothing of <term>abstinence: "Enthaltung," the practice of refraining from sexual relations within marriage to focus on spiritual growth</term>? **Answer:** For that person, it is the hardest **cross** on earth to be bound to such a spirit. However, one partner can save the other and, with **JESUS'** assistance, may well heal them of this sickness—provided that the sick partner is willing to be helped and does not thrust **JESUS**, the **heavenly physician**, out of the house, and thus willfully separates, <note>original: "vid." - Latin for "see"</note> see *1 Corinthians 7:15, 16*. The herbs grow for the afflicted soul, who over the...

# Appendix

...must practice chastity in her own little garden, which **JESUS** keeps in her heart, *Song of Solomon 4:12*, in which there are all kinds of spice-beds, *Song of Solomon 6:1*. These were planted by our heavenly Physician against every <term>evil: <note>original: "Malum"</note></term> of the corrupted <term>tincture: <note>In theosophical and alchemical traditions used by Pietist writers, the "tincture" refers to the fundamental spiritual essence or life-force that animates a human being.</note></term> which was poisoned by the serpent in both man and woman. Faith, love, and hope bloom anew every morning; they are purely divine powers through which one constantly anticipates what is good and can allow nothing evil to enter the soul. By these means, the wicked poison is fundamentally removed from the sick person, and Satan is cast out. Faith is a <term>corrosive: <note>original: "Corrosiv"; used here as a medical metaphor for a substance that cleanses a wound by removing dead or infected tissue.</note></term> and a wine with which the wound is washed, and the love of **JESUS** in our heart is oil, which has the property of drawing out the evil completely and healing the wound. Hope is the bandage with which the sick person is bound.

The Divine Samaritan is provided with these <term>universal remedies: <note>original: "Universalibus"</note></term> for every kind of fall. The <term>specific details: <note>original: "Specialia"</note></term> He leaves to us, as if in the inn and <term>dwelling: <note>original: "Domicilio"</note></term> where spouses must tend to one another. To this end, the herbs and spices of divine <term>experience: <note>original: "Experientz"</note></term> are ready at hand in the garden, from which this simple eye-salve has also been <term>prepared: <note>original: "præpariret"</note></term>. This was not done with the intention of writing these things for the whole world or giving our "holy things to the dogs," etc., lest they tear us to pieces, *Matthew 7:6*. One is cautious with such matters; it is written only for ourselves, as a <term>home-remedy: <note>original: "Haus-Recept"</note></term>. Other good hearts ha-

<header>On the Married State.</header>

[they] also have their gift from God and have no need to be taught by men; dear <term>necessity<note>original: "Noht"; refers here to a spiritual pressing need or distress that compels a soul to seek divine help.</note></term> teaches them to pray for themselves, as it is said: "Seek, and you shall find," and the more one seeks, the more one finds, as experience teaches the soul. In this state, one does not wish to withhold the good that God has given us from our dear neighbor, but gladly shares what one is and has; all in God and His holy will. At the same time, however, we remind and warn you to likewise deal with this prudently and holily in the fear of God, and to distinguish between those who are wise or foolish <note>A reference to the Parable of the Ten Virgins in Matthew 25:1-13, used here as a metaphor for spiritual readiness.</note> virgins, and to send the latter to the apothecaries or merchants who keep their wares for sale publicly; there they may provide for themselves.

16. May the Lord keep us in His holy fear, and give us all a mind and will in Christ, which consents to no sin, nor can sin any more, for we are thereby born of God, *Tobit 4:6; 1 John 3:9*. May the dear God also make us all strong in spirit, so that in this will in Christ we may cast down and bind Satan, and thus lay him before the feet of **JESUS**, to crush the head of the serpent, Amen! yes

chastity must be maintained in her own little garden, which **Jesus** has in her heart, <term>Song of Solomon 4:12 <note>original: "Cant. 4: 12"</note></term> wherein there are all kinds of little spice-beds, <term>Chapter 6:1 <note>original: "Cap. 6. 1."</note></term> which our heavenly Physician has planted against every <term>evil <note>original: "Malum"</note></term> of the corrupted <term>tincture <note>original: "Tinctur"; in alchemical and mystical theology, the "tincture" refers to the essential spiritual quality or life-force that colors a person’s soul.</note></term> poisoned by the serpent, both in man and woman.
